  Effective social intelligence simulation requires language agents to dynamically adjust reasoning depth, a capability notably absent in current studies. Existing methods either lack explicit reasoning or employ lengthy Chain-of-Thought reasoning uniformly across all scenarios, resulting in excessive token usage and inflexible social behaviors in tasks such as negotiation or collaboration. To address this, we propose an $\textbf{A}$daptive $\textbf{S}$ocial $\textbf{L}$earning ($\textbf{ASL}$) framework in this paper, aiming to improve the adaptive reasoning ability of language agents in dynamic social interactions. To this end, we first identify the hierarchical reasoning modes under such context, ranging from intuitive response to deep deliberation based on the cognitive control theory. We then develop the $\textbf{A}$daptive $\textbf{M}$ode $\textbf{P}$olicy $\textbf{O}$ptimization ($\textbf{AMPO}$) algorithm to learn the context-aware mode adaptation and reasoning. Our framework advances existing research in three key aspects: (1) Multi-granular reasoning mode design, (2) Context-aware mode switching in rich social interaction, and (3) Token-efficient reasoning with depth adaptation. Extensive experiments on the benchmark social intelligence environment verify that ASL achieves 15.6% higher task performance than GPT-4o. Notably, our AMPO outperforms GRPO by 7.0% with 32.8% shorter thinking chains, demonstrating the advantages of our AMPO and the learned adaptive reasoning ability over GRPO's solution.
  </details>

- **[$\texttt{SEM-CTRL}$: Semantically Controlled Decoding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.01804)**  `arXiv:2503.01804`  
  _Mohammad Albinhassan, Pranava Madhyastha, Alessandra Russo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Ensuring both syntactic and semantic correctness in Large Language Model (LLM) outputs remains a significant challenge, despite being critical for real-world deployment. In this paper, we introduce \texttt{SEM-CTRL}, a unified approach that allows for enforcing rich context-sensitive constraints, and task and instance specific semantics directly on the LLM decoder. Our approach integrates token-level MCTS which is guided by specific syntactic and semantic constraints. The constraints over desired outputs are expressed using Answer Set Grammars, which is a logic-based formalism that generalizes context sensitive grammars while incorporating background knowledge to represent task-specific semantics. We show that our approach helps guarantee valid completions for any off-the-shelf LLM without the need for fine-tuning. We evaluate \texttt{SEM-CTRL} on a range of tasks, including synthetic grammar synthesis, combinatorial reasoning, JSON parsing, and planning. Our experimental results demonstrate that \texttt{SEM-CTRL} allows even small pre-trained LLMs to efficiently outperform larger variants and state-of-the-art reasoning models (e.g., \textit{o4-mini}) while simultaneously guaranteeing semantic validity.
  </details>

- **[Make LoRA Great Again: Boosting LoRA with Adaptive Singular Values and Mixture-of-Experts Optimization Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.16894)**  `arXiv:2502.16894`  
  _Chenghao Fan, Zhenyi Lu, Sichen Liu, Chengfeng Gu, Xiaoye Qu, Wei Wei, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) enables parameter-efficient fine-tuning for Large Language Models (LLMs), its performance often falls short of Full Fine-Tuning (Full FT). Current methods optimize LoRA by initializing with static singular value decomposition (SVD) subsets, leading to suboptimal leveraging of pre-trained knowledge. Another path for improving LoRA is incorporating a Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) architecture. However, weight misalignment and complex gradient dynamics make it challenging to adopt SVD prior to the LoRA MoE architecture. To mitigate these issues, we propose \underline{G}reat L\underline{o}R\underline{A} Mixture-of-Exper\underline{t} (GOAT), a framework that (1) adaptively integrates relevant priors using an SVD-structured MoE, and (2) aligns optimization with full fine-tuned MoE by deriving a theoretical scaling factor. We demonstrate that proper scaling, without modifying the architecture or training algorithms, boosts LoRA MoE's efficiency and performance. Experiments across 25 datasets, including natural language understanding, commonsense reasoning, image classification, and natural language generation, demonstrate GOAT's state-of-the-art performance, closing the gap with Full FT.
  </details>

- **[Hallucination, Monofacts, and Miscalibration: An Empirical Investigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.08666)**  `arXiv:2502.08666`  
  _Miranda Muqing Miao, Michael Kearns_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Hallucinated facts in large language models (LLMs) have recently been shown to obey a statistical lower bound determined by the monofact rate (related to the classical Good-Turing missing mass estimator) minus model miscalibration (Kalai & Vempala, 2024). We present the first empirical investigation of this three-way relationship in classical n-gram models and fine-tuned encoder-decoder Transformers. By generating training data from Pareto distributions with varying shape parameters, we systematically control the monofact rates and establish its positive relationship with hallucination. To bridge theory and practice, we derive an empirical analog of the hallucination bound by replacing the population miscalibration term (Section 2.1) with an empirical bin-wise KL divergence and confirm its practical viability. We then introduce selective upweighting -- a simple yet effective technique that strategically repeats as little as 5% of training examples -- to deliberately inject miscalibration into the model. This intervention reduces hallucination by up to 40%, challenging universal deduplication policies. Our experiments reveal a critical trade-off: selective upweighting maintains pre-injection levels of accuracy while substantially reducing hallucination, whereas standard training gradually improves accuracy but fails to address persistently high hallucination, indicating an inherent tension in optimization objectives.
  </details>

- **[A Survey of Query Optimization in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17558)**  `arXiv:2412.17558`  
  _Mingyang Song, Mao Zheng_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Query Optimization (QO) has become essential for enhancing Large Language Model (LLM) effectiveness, particularly in Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) systems where query quality directly determines retrieval and response performance. This survey provides a systematic and comprehensive analysis of query optimization techniques with three principal contributions. \textit{First}, we introduce the \textbf{Query Optimization Lifecycle (QOL) Framework}, a five-phase pipeline covering Intent Recognition, Query Transformation, Retrieval Execution, Evidence Integration, and Response Synthesis, providing a unified lens for understanding the optimization process. \textit{Second}, we propose a \textbf{Query Complexity Taxonomy} that classifies queries along two dimensions, namely evidence type (explicit vs.\ implicit) and evidence quantity (single vs.\ multiple), establishing principled mappings between query characteristics and optimization strategies. \textit{Third}, we conduct an in-depth analysis of four atomic operations, namely \textbf{Query Expansion}, \textbf{Query Decomposition}, \textbf{Query Disambiguation}, and \textbf{Query Abstraction}, synthesizing a broad spectrum of representative methods from premier venues. We further examine evaluation methodologies, identify critical gaps in existing benchmarks, and discuss open challenges including process reward models, efficiency optimization, and multi-modal query handling. This survey offers both a structured foundation for research and actionable guidance for practitioners.
  </details>

- **[Diverging Preferences: When do Annotators Disagree and do Models Know?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.14632)**  `arXiv:2410.14632`  
  _Michael JQ Zhang, Zhilin Wang, Jena D. Hwang, Yi Dong, Olivier Delalleau, Yejin Choi, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We examine diverging preferences in human-labeled preference datasets. We develop a taxonomy of disagreement sources spanning ten categories across four high-level classes and find that the majority of disagreements are due to factors such as task underspecification or response style. Our findings challenge a standard assumption in reward modeling methods that annotator disagreements can be attributed to simple noise. We then explore how these findings impact two areas of LLM development: reward modeling training and evaluation. In our experiments, we demonstrate how standard reward modeling (e.g., Bradley-Terry) and LLM-as-Judge evaluation methods fail to account for divergence between annotators. These findings highlight challenges in LLM evaluations, which are greatly influenced by divisive features like response style, and in developing pluralistically aligned LLMs. To address these issues, we develop methods for identifying diverging preferences to mitigate their influence in evaluations and during LLM training.
  </details>

- **[Reproduction and Replication of an Adversarial Stylometry Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.07395)**  `arXiv:2208.07395`  
  _Haining Wang, Patrick Juola, Allen Riddell_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Maintaining anonymity in natural language communication remains a challenging task. Even when the number of candidate authors is large, standard authorship attribution techniques that analyze writing style predict the original author with uncomfortably high accuracy. Adversarial stylometry provides a defense against authorship attribution, helping users avoid unwanted deanonymization. This paper reproduces and replicates experiments from a seminal study of defenses against authorship attribution (Brennan et al., 2012). After reproducing the experiment using the original data, we then replicate the experiment by repeating the online field experiment using the procedures described in the original paper. Although we reach the same conclusion as the original paper, our results suggest that the defenses studied may be overstated in their effectiveness. This is largely due to the absence of a control group in the original study. In our replication, we find evidence suggesting that an entirely automatic method, round-trip translation, warrants re-examination because it appears to reduce the effectiveness of established authorship attribution methods.
  </details>

[↑ Back to Top](#-full-archive)

</details>

### Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition 📸

<details open><summary>Click to Collapse</summary>

- **[Rethinking Video Autoencoders for Interactive World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30129)**  `arXiv:2610.30129`  
  _Adrien Ramanana Rahary, Yutong Bai, Nicolas Dufour, Patrick Pérez, David Picard_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video world models predict future frames from past frames and actions. They serve as simulators in which agents train, plan or are evaluated, and that humans can control interactively. They generate in the latent space of a video autoencoder, whose compression sets their cost per frame and how long each action waits to take effect. Yet world models often reuse the autoencoders of text-to-video generators, built for offline generation with no agent waiting and, without their training data and code, not retrainable at another compression. We therefore ask how, where and how much a world model should compress. We build CADRE, an open family of causal diffusion representation autoencoders spanning multiple spatio-temporal compressions. Its members differ only in a small bottleneck on frozen features and share a diffusion decoder self-distilled to one or a few steps. We train the same world model on each autoencoder in two domains, manipulation and navigation, with two environments each. CADRE forms most of the latency-quality frontier, especially with a moving camera. Our study also leads to four findings. At strong compression, a diffusion decoder is sharper than perceptual regression. In robot manipulation, temporal compression is more effective in the autoencoder than in the world model. Manipulation and navigation call for opposite compressions: temporal compression costs little when the camera is still, and spatial compression when it moves. Surprisingly, with a still camera, coarser spatial tokens hurt motion far more than image quality. Code, models and a live demo are atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[VoxProg: 3D Spatial Reasoning through Voxel Programming](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30127)**  `arXiv:2610.30127`  
  _Zhichao Zhai, Menglei Zhao, Guikun Chen, Zihao Yan, Kanzhi Wu, Wenguan Wang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Language models' abilities to interpret tasks and generate programs offer a promising basis for 3D spatial reasoning. Yet following spatial instructions in 3D scenes requires programs to construct geometric references that language leaves implicit. In this work, we introduce VoxProg, which formulates 3D object placement through voxel programming: a language model translates task interpretations into executable programs over local voxel views of scene surfaces. Because these views index surface geometry on regular grids with explicit coordinates and neighborhood relations, an evidence program can isolate task-relevant regions and boundaries directly from scene meshes, and a downstream pose program can measure the resulting geometry to compute a target $SE(3)$ pose. Visual and numerical feedback from execution lets the model inspect intermediate selections and candidate placements to iteratively revise its code. To evaluate open-ended placement across functional use, geometric fit, and contextual arrangements, we introduce the 71-task VoxProg-Bench benchmark along with an evaluation suite pairing perceptual Intent Alignment and 3D Physical Validity. Across VoxProg-Bench and external benchmarks, VoxProg consistently outperforms relation-based, keypoint-based, and mesh-based baselines.
  </details>

- **[ALPS: Visual State Encoders for World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30120)**  `arXiv:2610.30120`  
  _Yanqing Liu, Letian Zhang, Chengzhi Liu, Zijun Wang, Jieru Mei, Hongliang Fei, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  World models turn visual representations into predictions and actions. We argue that their visual foundation should combine physical-state information, native language alignment, and pixel reconstruction, so that a single representation can support prediction, instruction, and inspection. However, existing encoders typically specialize in only one or two of these capabilities; we therefore introduce ALPS, a pretrained visual state encoder that unifies all three in a single set of patch tokens. Using only still images and text, we pretrain a ViT-B/16 from scratch with language, reconstruction, and spatial correspondence objectives, and then refine it via cross-view self-distillation. In a unified evaluation across fifteen public encoders, ALPS achieves the lowest aggregate LIBERO state-probe error among ViT-B models (matching the 3.5x larger V-JEPA 2-L) while maintaining strong language alignment and accurate pixel reconstruction. Downstream, frozen ALPS tokens reach the highest mean policy success on LIBERO (85.5%) when paired with our native text tower, match or exceed leading encoders on world-model planning across PushT, Wall, and Granular, and allow predicted future tokens to be decoded directly into pixels for visual inspection. We hope this work helps establish visual pretraining as a shared foundation for understanding, prediction, and action in world models.
  </details>

- **[ProactEgoMem: Benchmarking Proactive Memory in Streaming Egocentric Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30089)**  `arXiv:2610.30089`  
  _Ruotong Liao, Meikel Kokowski, Xiaoxiao Sun, Yongqin Xian, Laura Bravo Sánchez, Shiye Su, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Today's multimodal assistants are typically prompted to interpret the visual evidence presented to them. To offload the cognitive burden of remembering across long horizons, wearable assistants should instead proactively acquire and maintain useful memory as the user's surroundings evolve. Doing so on the user's behalf requires identifying what matters, understanding the scene in real time, and initiating memory maintenance in the background while the user stays focused on their activities. We introduce ProactEgoMem, a benchmark that evaluates proactive memory in streaming egocentric video. Three tasks ground the evaluation in ambient environmental needs: Information Capture (IC) preserves transient yet dense visual information, State Tracking (ST) follows changing object states, and Location Tracking (LT) records object movements. The benchmark defines its protocol at two levels of proactiveness: the Notification Level asks for a single timely notification that satisfies the user's intent, while the deeper Intervention Level asks the agent to maintain memory on the user's behalf through an active start-to-stop tool lifecycle. Parallel ProactEgoMem-Daily and ProactEgoMem-Sci tracks contain 300/100 clips covering daily and scientific use cases, paired with 1000/500 questions in ProactEgoMem-QA, which measures the quality of the proactively acquired memory. Across proprietary, open-source, video-language, and streaming baselines, even the strongest models fail to capture memory both timely and accurately. ProactEgoMem provides a rigorous evaluation foundation for proactive multimodal assistance and for video agents that co-exist with their users.
  </details>

- **[Attn-Forcing: Learning Full-Context Attention Proxies for Streaming Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30036)**  `arXiv:2610.30036`  
  _Junyao Gao, Sibo Liu, Jiaxing Li, Yanan Sun, Hanxin Zhu, Weidong Zhang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Increasing the attention context length significantly improves streaming video generation quality, while incurring prohibitive memory and computational costs as the context grows. Existing methods attempt to capture this contextual information through attention selection or compression, but remain limited by bounded representations and lack explicit attention-level supervision to retain historical contributions to current queries. To address this, we introduce Attn-Forcing, a novel framework that preserves full-context attention contributions for streaming video generation under bounded memory and computation. We achieve this by introducing attention proxies that continuously approximate the attention responses induced by the historical context. Specifically, we implement the proxies as learnable visual latents and employ a Proxy Update Module to incorporate visual context from historical latents. Moreover, we propose an Attention Matching Loss to explicitly constrain the attention behavior of the learned proxies using full-context attention supervision. Extensive qualitative and quantitative experiments demonstrate that Attn-Forcing consistently improves streaming video generation quality and achieves state-of-the-art performance over existing baseline methods.
  </details>

- **[Plausible but Unimplantable: A Trustworthiness Audit of Shape Completion Foundation Models for Cranial Defect Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30035)**  `arXiv:2610.30035`  
  _Marek Wodzinski, Kamil Kwarciak, Daria Hemmerling, Mateusz Daniol_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  A patient who has lost part of the skull vault needs an implant that fits the defect exactly, and such implants are still drawn by hand in CAD by specialists, which makes each case slow and expensive. The task can be considered as a shape completion problem, so pretrained completion models, and now the first shape completion foundation models, look like a way to remove that manual step. We audited three publicly released pretrained shape completion models, two of them foundation models, applied zero-shot to 239 cranial defects, some carved from public skull datasets and some real post-craniectomy cases. Each output was compared with simply returning the patient's own remaining bone, and tested for whether the predicted material could be placed without intersecting the bone that is still there. On the 210 carved defects none of the three improved on doing nothing, and on the 29 real cases only one did so beyond chance. Yet not one output, from any model in any configuration, was implantable: none managed both to stay clear of the bone the patient still has and to place more material than doing nothing, where a task-specific volumetric model, trained for this task and selected on these cases, was implantable on all but one. The output that most convincingly resembles a skull is no more placeable than the rest, and at these point densities implant thickness cannot be read off the emitted points at all, which is a property of the output representation rather than of any one model. On this evidence the shape completion foundation models we could obtain cannot be trusted and, used as released, are not usable in clinical practice. Substantial research towards trustworthy foundation models for shape completion is still needed.
  </details>

- **[RealMe: Immersive Volumetric Video from Minimal Captures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30023)**  `arXiv:2610.30023`  
  _Chenxu Zhou, Zhiyuan Yu, Yudong Jin, Yinji Shentu, Yu Zhang, Jiaming Sun, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  This paper addresses the problem of creating immersive volumetric videos from sparse and low-resolution capture resources. Conventional dense multi-view reconstruction methods can produce high-quality volumetric videos, but their reliance on costly capture setups makes them impractical in real-world scenarios. Recent approaches use generative models to relax capture requirements, but still suffer from limited resolution and 4D inconsistency. To overcome these constraints, we propose RealMe, a unified framework built around a high-resolution video diffusion model that enhances limited captures into high-resolution (2K), 4D-consistent videos for volumetric video reconstruction. Specifically, RealMe constructs auxiliary data from capture videos and encodes them into latents along with layout-aware positional embeddings. The video diffusion model adapts factorized spatio-temporal attention to the multi-view capture layout, denoises these latents across viewpoints and time, and decodes them into enhanced videos to refine the 4D representation. This adaptation helps the model capture temporal dynamics and multi-view relationships, leading to more 4D-consistent generation. To reduce the data gap between generated videos and real captures, we apply a latent frequency loss that provides frequency-domain supervision in latent space. Extensive experiments on the public DNA-Rendering benchmark and our self-captured RealVV dataset demonstrate that RealMe is able to create immersive volumetric videos and outperforms state-of-the-art methods in visual fidelity and 4D consistency.
  </details>

- **[ClunoGS: Compact Language 3D Gaussian Splatting via Unified Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30013)**  `arXiv:2610.30013`  
  _Jian Xu, Yi Li, Ying Huang, Siyi Wu, Bingzhe Li, Yuede Ji, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Language 3D Gaussian Splatting enables open-vocabulary scene understanding by associating Gaussian primitives with language features. These semantic attributes increase model storage, while most existing 3DGS compression methods are designed mainly to preserve RGB rendering quality. One straightforward approach is to apply sparsification and quantization sequentially. However, pruning based only on rendering quality can remove Gaussians that have small RGB contributions but are important for semantic prediction. Once removed, their semantic information cannot be recovered by later quantization. We propose ClunoGS, a compact framework for Language 3DGS that combines semantics-aware Gaussian sparsification and vector quantization in a unified constrained objective. Semantic supervision is used during sparsification, and the objective jointly considers rendering quality, semantic accuracy, and compactness. We optimize the unified objective with alternating primal, sparsification, quantization, and dual updates. We evaluate ClunoGS on open-vocabulary localization and segmentation using the LERF and ScanNet-10 benchmarks. Under aggressive compression, our unified, semantics-aware formulation substantially alleviates the degradation of semantic representations caused by sequential compression and semantics-agnostic Gaussian sparsification. Ablations further show that jointly coordinating sparsification and quantization better preserves semantic accuracy than applying them independently.
  </details>

- **[Multiclass Boundary Refinement with Neural Cellular Automata for Remote Sensing Land Cover Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30007)**  `arXiv:2610.30007`  
  _Pascoassis Souza Santos Meira, Gilson Antonio Giraldi, Rafael Duarte Coelho dos Santos_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Semantic segmentation of remote sensing imagery often places class boundaries a few pixels away from where they truly lie, and this position error is concentrated in the thin band measured by Boundary IoU (BIoU). We treat boundary refinement as the iterated application of a small learned local rule: the Boundary Refinement Neural Cellular Automaton (BRNCA), that starts from the class probabilities of a frozen, pre-trained segmenter and corrects them cell by cell. We generalise the binary rNCA to the multiclass case by keeping the visible channels on the probability simplex through an Aitchison (log-space) update and by adding free hidden channels. The best configuration improves the mean BIoU over the base segmenter by 2.36 points (from 46.63 to 48.99). The gain is concentrated on the irregular woodland class (+8.5 points BIoU); this suggests that position error is correlated with the fractal dimension of the class boundaries.
  </details>

- **[Looking Ahead: Modeling Future Evolution in Autoregressive Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29989)**  `arXiv:2610.29989`  
  _Ruopeng Gao, Yukun Huang, Jiarui Hu, Shuai Wang, Wenbo Hu, Ying Shan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Autoregressive video diffusion models generate videos chunk by chunk, yet producing plausible continuations does not guarantee that they capture how scenes evolve. Under teacher forcing, strong temporal redundancy may allow local visual continuity to serve as a shortcut for current-chunk denoising, reducing the pressure to model visual evolution. We introduce Future Chunk Prediction (FCP), an auxiliary objective jointly optimized with teacher-forced denoising that predicts temporally separated chunks without access to the intervening visual context. Drawing supervision directly from training videos, FCP requires no additional pretrained visual encoder and leaves autoregressive inference unchanged. Our method achieves superior physical plausibility over standard teacher-forced autoregressive training with a lower overall training compute budget, highlighting the effectiveness and efficiency of future-chunk supervision for modeling visual evolution. Intriguingly, our visualizations suggest that FCP fosters a more structured representation space, which may be valuable for building autoregressive world models.
  </details>

- **[Training-free Acceleration for Blind Compressed Image Restoration via Activation-Calibrated Singular Value Decomposition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29959)**  `arXiv:2610.29959`  
  _Suhang Yao, Xin Li, Xijun Wang, Yufei Wang, Yeying Jin, Zhibo Chen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Transformer-based models achieve strong performance on blind compressed image restoration (CIR) under unknown compression settings, but their densely stacked projection operators make deployment costly. We study training-free acceleration for such models through an empirical, operator-level analysis. Specifically, we profile where computation is concentrated, characterize channel-wise activation distributions under compressed inputs, and examine the singular-value decay of representative projection weights. These measurements identify the recurring projections in the attention, feed-forward, convolutional, and prompt-conditioning pathways as effective low-rank replacement targets that preserve the original inference graph. They further establish truncated SVD (TSVD) as a principled training-free baseline for blind CIR and reveal why a uniform low-rank objective is mismatched to the channel-imbalanced feature distributions of compressed images. Accordingly, we formulate Activation-Calibrated Singular Value Decomposition (ASVD) for this restoration setting, deriving low-rank factors entirely offline from pretrained weights and lightweight activation statistics gathered from only a few compressed calibration images. By weighting the decomposition with channel-wise activation magnitudes, our ASVD assigns larger penalties to errors on more active channels, requiring neither retraining nor online calibration and leaving the restoration pipeline intact. When instantiated on PromptCIR at rank 64, ASVD consistently improves PSNR-B over TSVD across four standard benchmarks under identical parameter and MAC budgets. At this operating point, the factorized model cuts parameters by 59% (34.75M to 14.34M) and computation by 16.1% (258.79 to 217.16 GMACs for a 256 x 256 input), enabling acceleration without retraining.
  </details>

- **[DewHand: Direct Metric World-Space Hand Motion Reconstruction from Egocentric Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29936)**  `arXiv:2610.29936`  
  _Naru Suzuki, Satoshi Ikehata, Takehiko Ohkawa, Yoichi Sato, Shuhei Kurita_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recovering metric world-space hand motion from egocentric video supports understanding human interactions and transferring them to embodied agents. Three challenges complicate this task: multi-stage pipelines propagate errors from independently estimated hand and camera motion; monocular observations leave physical scale ambiguous; and occlusion and out-of-view hands admit multiple plausible motion trajectories. We present DewHand, a unified model for direct joint reconstruction of world-space hand and camera motion. To avoid compounding errors from multi-stage estimation, DewHand jointly predicts hand and camera motion in a shared world coordinate system within a single-stage pipeline. To resolve metric-scale ambiguity, it combines scene-level geometric features from VGGT-$\Omega$ with metric-space supervision, while hand-specific features preserve fine-grained articulation. To handle occlusion and out-of-view hands, we formulate reconstruction as conditional motion generation with flow matching. Under the same architecture, this generative formulation outperforms direct regression in both accuracy and temporal smoothness with only two sampling steps. Experiments on HOT3D and ARCTIC demonstrate improved world-space accuracy and temporal consistency over state-of-the-art methods. DewHand reduces rigid-aligned world-space error relative to StableHand from 32.9 to 28.4 mm on HOT3D and from 76.2 to 36.2 mm on ARCTIC. It also recovers camera scale within 10% on two thirds of HOT3D clips, versus about a quarter for SLAM. Moreover, in zero-shot evaluations on HoloAssist, H2O, and TACO, captured with different devices, DewHand achieves the lowest rigid-aligned world-space error (WA-MPJPE) on each dataset.
  </details>

- **[CausalAlign: Causal Alignment of Video Generation Models for Consistent Ego-Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29915)**  `arXiv:2610.29915`  
  _Kai Zhu, Yintianrun Zhang, Shuwei Jiang, Ruolin Zuo, Yixin Chen, Duomin Wang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion-based video generation models (VGMs) can produce visually plausible videos, yet their camera ego-motion often drifts over long horizons. Camera ego-motion determines how the viewpoint changes between frames and is essential for maintaining consistent geometry and trajectories. To address this problem, we propose CausalAlign, a causal alignment framework with two components. First, we diagnose how precise ego motion is represented, used, and retained within VGMs using linear probes and causal interventions, revealing a rise-and-fall pattern during denoising. Second, we strengthen ego-motion state in VGM by aligning the representations at the identified layers to a 3D foundation model through delta-token space. This space encodes the changes between consecutive frames, allowing the alignment to focus on ego-motion dynamics rather than static scene content. With Wan2.2 fine-tuned on DL3DV and RealEstate10K, CausalAlign can largely improve motion accuracy, geometric consistency, and temporal smoothness, while preserving visual quality. For example, it improves geometric consistency by more than 20% relative to the base model. Moreover, with the fine-tuned VGM, CausalAlign improves the success rate of world action models (WAMs) by 10 points on mobile manipulation tasks of RoboCasa365 benchmark. Overall, CausalAlign improves ego-motion consistency in diffusion-based VGMs and yields noticeable gains in embodied models.
  </details>

- **[Asking a Multimodal LLM Whether Someone Fell: Zero-Shot Screening of Low-Resolution Thermal Video in a Laboratory and an Occupied Home](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29913)**  `arXiv:2610.29913`  
  _Marcos Junca, Mauro Junca_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Thermal cameras can watch over older people living alone without capturing faces, and multimodal large language models (LLMs) can judge a short video without task-specific training. We evaluate a production multimodal LLM, Gemini 3.8 Flash at temperature 0, as the decision stage of an event-triggered fall screener, used exactly as a deployed prototype uses it: one request per short thermal clip, with its sound track when there is one, and its predecessor Gemini 3.7 Flash on the same requests. On 438 staged falls and 563 non-fall windows that we located by hand in a public 32x24 thermal dataset, the deployed request called 69% of the held-out subjects' falls (67% over all subjects; bed falls excluded) and none of 499 non-fall windows, including people lying on the floor and getting up. Over 76 h of ordinary life in an occupied room, recorded at 160x120 with sound, it called none of 395 clips a fall (95% upper bound 1.2 false alarms per day) and caught 4 of the 9 staged falls the camera could see. One cautionary sentence in the prompt ("a person who goes out of view ... is not a fall") accounted for many of the misses: removing it raised sensitivity on the held-out subjects from 69% to 83% on 3.8 Flash and from 80% to 92% on Gemini 3.7 Flash, and in the home from 4 to 9 of the 9 visible falls, with no false call in ordinary living but at the price of calling deliberate lie-downs a fall. Sensitivity collapsed at 8x6 pixels (34%); identical requests disagreed on 4% of windows; and when the model called a fall, its explanation placed it within a second of the annotated landing 99% of the time. Two conventional detectors trained on the laboratory data caught nearly every held-out laboratory fall but raised 14.2 and 23.9 false alarms per day in the home. We release the frame-level fall annotations of the public dataset and every model answer on it.
  </details>

- **[ViBMR: A Large-Scale Multi-Sequence Brain MRI Dataset and Comprehensive Evaluation for Vietnamese Radiology Report Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29905)**  `arXiv:2610.29905`  
  _Dai Lam Le, Minh Khoi Nguyen, Son Dinh Nguyen, Amir Reza Jafari, Viet Ha, Nguyen Son, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Automated radiology report generation for multi-sequence brain MRI holds strong promise for alleviating radiologists' workload, yet it remains a demanding problem: brain MRI studies are inherently volumetric, each pulse sequence carries complementary diagnostic information, and annotated data are critically scarce for low-resource clinical languages. Existing brain MRI-report resources are still dominated by English reports, translated clinical text, or datasets adapted from task-specific imaging benchmarks, leaving routine low-resource clinical reporting and heterogeneous multi-sequence MRI protocols insufficiently represented. To bridge this gap, we introduce ViBMR, to the best of our knowledge, the first large-scale multi-sequence brain MRI dataset paired with routine Vietnamese clinical reports, comprising 3,649 patients and 11,875 MRI series across T1, T2, FLAIR, and contrast-enhanced T1 sequences. Building on ViBMR, we establish a comprehensive evaluation setup for brain MRI report generation, covering representative medical vision-language models, multi-sequence inputs, and feature aggregation strategies. The results provide key insights into dataset characteristics and model behavior, while demonstrating the necessity of native low-resource clinical datasets for reliable Vietnamese-origin brain MRI report generation. Our dataset and baseline models will be released for non-commercial research.
  </details>

- **[Expandable Modality Experts for Medical Vision-Language Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29881)**  `arXiv:2610.29881`  
  _DM BAPPY, Eunsu Kim, Hanjoo Lee, Sung Hak Lee, Sangjeong Ahn_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Learning across medical imaging modalities requires making use of images without paired text and accommodating new domains while retaining existing representations. We propose a medical vision-language technique built around an expandable bank of modality experts. Its Multiway Transformer combines shared visual computation with deterministically routed feed-forward experts and a separate text pathway. A modality-aware visual tokenizer supplies targets for masked pretraining on 19.4 million unpaired image rows across seven modalities; contrastive alignment then uses 3.23 million image-text pairs. Additional fundus and optical coherence tomography experts learn from paired data while the original image and text embedding paths remain frozen. With a ViT-Large backbone, the proposed technique achieves the best or tied-best result in 24 of 25 benchmark settings across nine modalities: 10 of 11 zero-shot classification benchmarks, all 10 linear probes, and all four retrieval benchmarks. Mean balanced accuracy is 0.674 for zero-shot classification and 0.856 for linear probing, and mean bidirectional retrieval recall is 20.70%. A separate three-seed sequential experiment with matched adaptation data yields fundus and OCT retrieval gains of 14.95 and 9.31 percentage points, with no measured loss on preceding fundus or pathology tasks. The preservation argument requires unchanged existing computation paths and inference conditions, and feature-identity checks verify retention on evaluated inputs. The proposed technique provides a modular approach to learning and expanding medical vision-language representations.
  </details>

- **[Stochastic Nonlinearities Improve Uncertainty Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29875)**  `arXiv:2610.29875`  
  _Mohammad Sarhangzadeh, Amirreza M. Shebly, Doruk Oner_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reliable uncertainty estimation is important across visual recognition tasks, providing information about the confidence and reliability of model predictions. Many uncertainty estimation methods obtain predictive diversity through mechanisms that can suppress intermediate features, perturb the input, or require substantial additional computation. Stochastic activation functions, where network units randomly select between multiple nonlinearities during a forward pass, have previously been used to improve optimization behavior, mitigate limitations of fixed ReLU activations, and increase output diversity. In this work, we reframe stochastic activation switching as a lightweight mechanism for uncertainty estimation. The proposed method samples between nonlinear activation functions, inducing predictive diversity while keeping intermediate feature pathways active. This enables uncertainty estimation from a single trained model without the training cost of ensembles, task-specific uncertainty heads, or architectural redesign. We evaluate the proposed approach across classification, semantic segmentation, and out-of-distribution detection using convolutional and transformer-based architectures on diverse datasets. Across these settings, stochastic activation switching improves uncertainty quality while preserving competitive primary-task performance, showing that stochastic nonlinearities provide an effective and information-preserving source of predictive variation for visual recognition.
  </details>

- **[GeoMem-VLA: Internalizing 3D Scene Understanding into Temporal Memory for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29866)**  `arXiv:2610.29866`  
  _Qinfan Zhang, Yifan Luo, Haoyuan Shi, Guanlin Liu, Han Dong, Haozhe Shan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Geometric structure is essential for robotic manipulation and understanding the 3D world. Most VLA models rely on 2D representations of current observations, while geometry-aware approaches often require additional 3D information. However, geometry inferred independently from individual observations is often discarded rather than accumulated into a persistent task representation. Our key insight is that action generation needs a compact, task-relevant geometric abstraction that can be continuously updated, rather than a complete, renderable 3D reconstruction at every step. We introduce GeoMem-VLA, a VLA framework that internalizes explicit 3D supervision into dedicated geometric tokens and maintains them as temporal memory for action generation, without directly aligning the VLM's native visual embeddings with the teacher feature space. During training, a frozen geometry foundation model supervises learnable GeoPlan tokens through a geometric flow-matching objective over the current scene point map and future end-effector tracks. These tokens are integrated with the memory of observation representations, where historical information is retrieved, adaptively fused with current observations, and consolidated to retain event frames as the task progresses. At inference, the policy retrieves historical representations and fuses them with the current working representation to predict actions, without invoking the 3D teacher or explicitly reconstructing the scene. After benchmark-specific downstream adaptation, GeoMem-VLA achieves 98.9% average success on LIBERO and 91.10% / 91.24% on RoboTwin's clean/randomized settings. Across five real-world tasks, GeoMem-VLA achieves 78.0% average success. These results suggest that internalizing and remembering 3D scene understanding is a promising approach to robot control.
  </details>

- **[Revealing Hidden Failure Modes in Chest X-ray Classification via Spectral Domain Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29830)**  `arXiv:2610.29830`  
  _Samuel Halimi, Loic Themyr, Arnaud Abreu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deep learning models for chest X-ray anomaly detection remain vulnerable to subtle distributional shifts (e.g., acquisition technique, patient-related factors, and preprocessing). Traditional error analysis often relies on semantic metadata or model embeddings, which can mask low-level signal variations that degrade performance. In this work, we propose a data-centric framework for automated failure mode discovery using spectral analysis. We project images into the frequency domain and extract a compact profile summarizing the distribution of signal energy across frequency bands. By performing unsupervised clustering on these spectral profiles, we demonstrate that model failures are not randomly distributed, but are strongly concentrated within specific spectral clusters. This method effectively isolates "blind spots", enabling the prediction of model reliability and the discovery of performance-degrading data slices without requiring ground-truth failure annotations.
  </details>

- **[Early versus Late RGB-Infrared Fusion for Counter-UAV Detection: A Comparative Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29828)**  `arXiv:2610.29828`  
  _Alberto De Zan, Denis Tavaris, Gian Luca Foresti, Ivan Scagnetto_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  This paper presents a novel strategy for UAV detection through visible (RGB) and infrared (IR) fusion, in order to improve accuracy and precision in harsh environments and weather conditions. In this research, two opposite approaches have been studied and implemented: the first concerns image-level (early) fusion, which merges two different spectra into a single image to which one detector is applied. On the other hand, the second approach, called decision-level (late) fusion, focuses on blending together the output bounding boxes of two different detection pipelines, aiming to create a common result that can be applied to both spectra simultaneously. Both strategies have been extensively tested in a dedicated environment, which included alternating low and high brightness scenes, different backgrounds (sky and ground), distances (5, 25 and 60 m), altitudes and zoom levels (1X, 2X and 4X). These tests allowed for the assessment of actual detection capabilities and system reliability in real-world applications. Furthermore, the two fusion strategies were evaluated and compared over a set of aligned images in order to highlight the better approach of the two. The results indicate that early fusion achieves better raw performance, since it incorporates the thermal information from the beginning of the training process. However, late fusion presents an innovative and useful detection workflow that can be applied in cases of sensor or frame misalignment. Both positive and negative aspects have also been pointed out and discussed, in order to give a more comprehensive overview. Finally, the paper outlines implementation considerations and practical recommendations based on operational priorities. Overall, the findings contribute to the research in RGB-IR fusion modalities, with the goal of enhancing UAV detection capabilities.
  </details>

- **[GuidedSAM-Plume: An Expert-Guided Segmentation Framework for Volcanic Plumes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29825)**  `arXiv:2610.29825`  
  _Taddeo D'Adamo, Simon Thivet, Catherine Pothier, Riccardo Simionato, Allan Fries, Jonathan Lemus, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Quantitative analysis of environmental phenomena increasingly relies on image-based measurements. In volcanology, the geometric characterization of volcanic plumes and clouds from RGB images remains hindered by the scarcity of dedicated segmentation solutions and partially handcrafted annotations remain widely prevalent. We present GuidedSAM-Plume, an open-source expert-guided framework tailored for the interactive segmentation of volcanic plumes and clouds. It leverages the zero-shot, spatial prompt-based capabilities of SAM2. The framework was thoroughly validated on a challenging dataset through dedicated segmentation metrics and field-relevant volcanological descriptors. From minimal user inputs, it is able to significantly increase performance compared to fully automatic dedicated solutions while preserving the reliability of derived volcanological parameters. The absence of retraining requirements lowers adoption barriers within the volcanological community, and the framework's demonstrated suitability for complex, diffuse plume-shaped phenomena extends its applicability to broader environmental science domains. The re-annotated dataset, produced under the guidance of expert volcanologists within the proposed framework, ultimately yields a more standardized release. The framework and accompanying dataset are made available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Compositionality with Primitives: A Unified View from Euclidean to Hyperbolic Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29811)**  `arXiv:2610.29811`  
  _Puru Vaish, Meelad Dashti, Christoph Brune, Pascal Mettes, Tatiana Tommasi, Nicola Strisciuglio_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Compositional zero-shot learning (CZSL) aims to recognize novel combinations of previously observed objects and attributes. Prior work showed that large-scale vision-language models encode decomposable semantic structures that can be recomposed to enable generalization. The Geodesically Decomposable Embeddings (GDE) framework formalizes this by identifying directions in the tangent space at the Frechet mean of the embedding distribution, which encode primitive visual concepts. Unseen attribute-object compositions can be synthesized through vector addition of these primitives. We revisit the assumption that compositional generalization requires explicit attribute-object pair re-composition. By means of new compositional scoring functions and extensive evaluations, we show that directly using the decomposed primitives suffices for recognizing unseen compositions. In that, we gain insights on how the geometry of the embedding manifold (Euclidean, spherical, or hyperbolic) and dataset structure affect compositional reasoning. Across models (CLIP, DINOv2, MERU, HyCoCLIP), we show that compositional structure consistently emerges as primitive representations, enabling inference on CZSL benchmarks (MIT-States, VAW-CZSL, UT-Zappos50K, Clothing16K) and group robustness on Waterbirds and CelebA. Code:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Open-Vocabulary Models and Label Propagation for Uncertainty-aware Pseudo-Labeling of Object Detection Datasets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29788)**  `arXiv:2610.29788`  
  _Jonathan Klees, Timo Heller, Matthias Rottmann_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Object detection models rely on large-scale annotated datasets that require substantial manual annotation effort. The labeling costs further increase when annotation uncertainty is captured through soft labels, as multiple annotations must be collected for each object. We address this challenge by introducing a semi-supervised framework for the uncertainty-aware annotation of object detection datasets. Our approach leverages open-vocabulary object detectors to generate region proposals and semantically meaningful object representations. Based on these representations, we construct an object-level similarity graph and propagate probabilistic label information. Our framework assigns soft labels to previously unlabeled objects, quantifying both aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty. We evaluate our method on four object detection benchmarks with soft-label annotations and demonstrate that high-quality annotations can be obtained with only a small fraction of manually labeled objects. Furthermore, we show that detectors trained on semi-automatically annotated data achieve performance comparable to models trained on fully manually annotated data. We achieve state-of-the-art downstream detection performance for the task of semi-supervised object detection on the COCO dataset, with 40.5% AP virtually matching the performance of the model trained on fully manually constructed data (40.9%) at only 1% of labeling costs. These results highlight the potential of open-vocabulary models and graph-based label propagation for efficient, accurate and uncertainty-aware pseudo-labeling.
  </details>

- **[JointLight: Human-Centric Video Relighting with Asynchronous RGB-Irradiance Joint Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29780)**  `arXiv:2610.29780`  
  _Henglei Lv, Bailin Deng, Xiaoqiang Liu, Yichen Wang, Haoxian Zhang, Pengfei Wan, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Understanding and modeling light transport in human-centric scenes is a longstanding challenge. While recent progress in diffusion models has boosted research in relighting, precise illumination control still lags, primarily due to the scarcity of diverse, high-quality, precisely labeled video relighting datasets. Existing end-to-end relighting approaches model light transport implicitly through neural networks, which substantially complicates controllability. In contrast, some works resort to path-traced irradiance cues as an explicit blueprint for light transport. However, such constructed irradiance cues are prone to artifacts and hinder generative quality when directly used as control signals. With the key observation that noise corruption removes high-frequency artifacts and preserves low-frequency light transport, we introduce an asynchronous joint-modeling paradigm that facilitates fine-grained illumination control. During two-stage training, we force the model to jointly denoise RGB and ground-truth irradiance frames with separate timesteps scheduling. A domain matching strategy is applied to the second stage to bridge the domain gap between irradiance cues and ground-truth irradiance maps. At inference time, we introduce an asynchronous hijacked generation pipeline that facilitates flexible illumination control. For training data, we develop a multi-illumination high-quality synthetic video dataset with paired irradiance maps using a powerful game engine. Trained exclusively on this dataset, our model exhibits robust generalization to real- world scenarios and outperforms prior approaches in both lighting fidelity and generative quality. The code and dataset will be publicly released.
  </details>

- **[Key Findings for Small UAS for Post-Disaster Damage Assessment from the CURSE'26 Exercise](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29777)**  `arXiv:2610.29777`  
  _Thomas Manzini, Stephen Johnson, Priyankari Perali, David Merrick, Robin Murphy_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The CLARKE building and road damage assessment AI classifier was incorporated into the Catastrophic Unmanned Remote Sensing Exercise (CURSE) 2026, which trained 23 small uncrewed aerial systems (sUAS) teams from state and local agencies and non-governmental organizations in Florida and Missouri on how to collect imagery for an incident command Remote Sensing Cell. Although CLARKE has been operationally deployed to two hurricanes (Debby and Helene), CURSE'26 presented an opportunity to evaluate the machine learning model with a larger set of never-before-seen orthomosaics. The results show that building and road damage assessment together ran in 3.5 minutes on average, and that building damage assessment achieved a Micro F1 of 0.834 with no manual adjustments to correct spatial misalignment and delivered that performance in both Florida and Missouri, demonstrating good geographic transfer. It also showed that road damage assessment performed poorly due to sensitivity to misalignment between road maps and the images, and to lighting and environmental conditions in an urban setting. The analysis of CURSE'26 presents five other findings of value to the sUAS community: that the use of sUAS and machine learning is now operationally normalized and accepted, sUAS teams in the field can improve classification accuracy by timing their flights, exercises are good operational tests but their value for evaluating learning models is less clear, edge computing is needed to overcome the wireless upload barrier, and the application of sUAS and AI to disaster response has ethical and legal ramifications for pilots and data managers.
  </details>

- **[UniRAP: Towards Unified Part-level Physical Affordance Reasoning and Actionable Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29768)**  `arXiv:2610.29768`  
  _Linfei Li, Ruining Hu, Lin Zhang, Zhong Wang, Fengyi Zhang, Ying Shen, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language perception has achieved impressive progress in aligning natural language with visual observations, yet grounding high-level semantics into part-level physical interaction remains challenging. To address this gap, we propose UniRAP, a unified model for inferring part-level physical affordances and mapping language instructions to actionable geometric representations. UniRAP formulates this problem as a conditional multimodal generation task, integrating visual inputs, textual instructions, and optional prompts into a shared spatiotemporal representation space through a unified interface token mechanism. To predict executable contact geometry, we introduce a Unified Affordance Decoder (UAD), which jointly performs object detection, part-level affordance segmentation, and 4-DoF interaction pose estimation by leveraging intermediate segmentation features. In addition, we propose a curriculum-based transfer training strategy that progressively adapts the model from general visual parsing to interaction-aware perception, improving data efficiency under limited textual annotations. Experiments show that UniRAP achieves state-of-the-art performance on referring expression segmentation, affordance grounding, and interaction pose estimation, while maintaining strong spatiotemporal consistency in dynamic video scenarios. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of UniRAP as a unified perception framework for language-guided physical manipulation.
  </details>

- **[LEAF-Mamba: Feature Adaptation and Fusion for Whole Slide Image Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29746)**  `arXiv:2610.29746`  
  _Lubin Gan, Jing Zhang, Mingjun Ma, Linhao Qu, Dachun Kai, Xiaoyan Sun_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Whole slide image (WSI) analysis enables computational diagnosis and prognosis, but gigapixel images and limited annotations make slide-level learning challenging. Multiple instance learning addresses this task by aggregating patch representations from frozen pretrained encoders. Recently, Mamba has enabled efficient modeling of long patch sequences. However, task supervision alone does not explicitly constrain information recovery during feature adaptation, while Mamba couples local processing with long-range propagation rather than retaining separate representations for fusion. Motivated by these challenges, we introduce LEAF-Mamba, a framework that jointly addresses latent evidence adaptation and fusion for frozen-feature WSI analysis. A reconstruction-regularized adapter learns compact, task-specific representations while reconstructing the input features. Parallel Mamba and convolutional streams then capture long-range dependencies and sequence-local interactions, with an input-dependent gate modulating their joint representation before slide-level pooling. Experiments across feature extractors demonstrate improved average performance over strong MIL baselines in both diagnostic classification and survival prediction. Component analyses further support the contributions of adaptation and fusion. These findings highlight the value of jointly designing feature transformation and contextual aggregation to translate pretrained representations into effective slide-level predictions.
  </details>

- **[APED-CXR: Adaptive Privileged Evidence Distillation for Chest X-Ray Grounding with Image-Only Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29740)**  `arXiv:2610.29740`  
  _Md Shihabul Islam Shovo, Nishi Kanta Paul, Jamil Fayyad_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Compact chest X-ray (CXR) models that localize findings suit deployment, but box annotations are limited and report-aware multimodal models are expensive at inference. We introduce APED-CXR, which distills report-informed spatial evidence from a frozen multimodal teacher into a student with 11.1M parameters that predicts diseases and boxes from shared evidence maps and requires the image alone at test time. During training, the teacher uses the radiograph, report, and queried class to evaluate regions proposed from the student's current evidence maps, and its support scores serve as training targets for the student's pooled evidence scores within these regions. Trained on PadChest and evaluated on held-out PadChest-GR, refreshing the student's proposals for teacher evaluation improves box-level localization F1 from 0.302 with fixed warm-up proposals to 0.338 (IoU >= 0.5; gain 0.036, 95% CI [0.022, 0.049]), while maintaining diagnostic AUC. This gain depends on the teacher checking current proposals: with equally many random boxes, a fixed dense box set, teacher-generated boxes, or report rules instead of the teacher, F1 stays near the fixed-proposal level, and proposals two refreshes old recover only part of the gain. On NIH ChestX-ray14, a secondary test set, box recall also rises from 0.423 to 0.447.
  </details>

- **[Learning to Measure and Diagnose: A Clinical-Dictionary Radiomic Bottleneck Model for Thyroid Nodule Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29738)**  `arXiv:2610.29738`  
  _Guibin Zhang, Weier Luo, Lei Cao_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Thyroid ultrasound supports the assessment of nodule malignancy, but classification predictions often lack evidence linked to familiar clinical descriptions. Radiomics provides explicitly defined measurements that can support such explanations through a clinical dictionary. However, using these measurements as interpretable evidence requires reliable estimation from ultrasound images and preservation of measurement fidelity when optimizing classification performance. We propose a clinical-dictionary radiomic bottleneck model for thyroid nodule classification. Within this model, Family-Aware Radiomic Learning (FARL) estimates measurements with balanced supervision across feature families. Fidelity-Guided Bottleneck Modeling (FGBM) constructs a fixed bottleneck, followed by joint optimization of measurement estimation and classification under fidelity guidance. Decision evidence decomposition then traces feature contributions through a linear classifier and organizes them by clinical dictionary categories. Experiments on ThyroidXL and TN3K show competitive classification performance, while case studies illustrate feature contributions for benign and malignant nodules. These findings indicate interpretable classification with inspectable quantitative evidence informed by clinical knowledge. Code is available online.
  </details>

- **[Forward-Only Learning for Shared-Kernel Wavelet Neural Networks without Backpropagation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29697)**  `arXiv:2610.29697`  
  _Yongfei Guo, Xuan Sun, Ang Li, Zhao Zhang, Yuanhao Gong_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Backpropagation remains the dominant training paradigm, using backward gradients and local updates that can suffer from vanishing or exploding gradients in deep networks. Although the Forward-Forward algorithm avoids conventional backpropagation, it remains mainly local or layer-wise, motivating global whole-network learning using forward evaluations only. For shared-kernel wavelet neural networks, we therefore propose a CMA-ES-based global forward-only learning framework. We further introduce structure-aware parameter decomposition and channel-specific credit assignment, factorizing the search into three subspaces. On BSDS500, our method reduces test MSE from 4.1783 to 4.0130 and improves PSNR from 42.621 to 42.787~dB over Adam/BP without backward propagation. Beyond these gains, training requires only 6 training-set coverages, compared with 1500 epochs for BP. Ablations show that structural decomposition and aligned credit assignment are more important than increasing population size, demonstrating the feasibility of whole-network forward-only learning for compact reconstruction networks. These results suggest a potential route toward forward-only optimization of compact reconstruction models for resource-constrained imaging and edge or near-sensor systems.
  </details>

- **[Efficient Generative Image Compression with Quantized Latents Refinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29691)**  `arXiv:2610.29691`  
  _Hao Cao, Yinhuan Huang, Wenqi Guo, Zhijin Qin, Jungong Han_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generative image compression (GIC) delivers strong perceptual quality at low bitrates. However, GIC methods with strong perceptual quality often rely on pretrained diffusion models with high latency, while recent lightweight GIC methods reduce latency but deliver lower perceptual quality. We observe that, for lightweight GIC methods at low bitrates, coarse deterministic quantization can collapse nearby encoder latents corresponding to different fine details to the same point, making these details difficult to recover. Motivated by this, we propose GR-GIC, an efficient GIC that delivers competitive reconstruction performance with fast inference. For better performance, we introduce Guided Refinement (GR), which combines latent refinement guided by entropy context with adaptive perturbations for training regularization, jointly improving reconstruction at low bitrates. For fast inference, we introduce reorganized projected residual vector quantization (P-RVQ), which equivalently reorganizes residual updates into low-dimensional lookups to expose greater parallelism and reduce sequential computation. Experiments on Kodak show that for performance, compared with OneDC, GR-GIC achieves BD-rate savings of 23.19%, 37.71%, 22.30%, and 22.14% for LPIPS, DISTS, PSNR, and FID, respectively. For fast inference, the highly optimized implementation of GR-GIC encodes and decodes in 4.60 and 5.98 ms on an NVIDIA A100. Specifically, the reorganized P-RVQ implementation achieves a $3.14\times$ average speedup over the original implementation under the same FP32 setting. On an NVIDIA Jetson Orin NX, GR-GIC achieves latency comparable to lightweight GIC methods.
  </details>

- **[Rethinking Post-hoc Debiasing of Large Pretrained Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29688)**  `arXiv:2610.29688`  
  _Divyaansh Mertia, Chirag Bhutra, Himanshu Shekhar, Shubham Bagwari, Pratik Mazumder_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large pretrained models such as CLIP are widely used as frozen feature extractors. However, their embeddings inherit spurious correlations from training data, introducing bias into the features. A growing body of research has sought to address this issue through post hoc debiasing, leaving the backbone untouched. We discover that despite differing mechanisms, these methods require the bias feature to be identified and expressed in text. This dependency is not innocuous. In this work, we experimentally demonstrate that this requirement incurs an unforeseen penalty in certain scenarios. For example, on the UrbanCars benchmark, having two co-occurring bias attributes, the recent methods underperform a vanilla classifier on biased frozenbackbone features, and even providing ground-truth bias identities does not close the gap. We show that the pattern that such methods seek is readily observable in the frozen geometry. Specifically, cross-class training pairs placed closest together by the frozen encoder almost always share a spurious attribute, regardless of the bias type, without requiring access to the bias labels. Based on this, we propose a novel approach to identify such pairs and learn a single linear map that transforms the features to perform post-hoc debiasing. The proposed approach achieves competitive performance across all datasets without access to bias attribute annotations or any LLMs.
  </details>

- **[Learning to Reason Like a Cytopathologist:Clinically Grounded Multiscale Evidential Learning for Cervical Cytology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29665)**  `arXiv:2610.29665`  
  _Ajeet Kumar Singh, Nehal Mishra, Anurag Tripathi, Santanu Chaudhury, Dorien Herremans_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cervical cytology presents a fundamentally ambiguous diagnostic landscape, where equivocal categories such as Atypical Squamous Cells of Undetermined Significance (ASC-US) occupy a morphological grey zone that is definitional. For clinical decision support, the goal extends beyond assigning a definitive label to every sample: cases must also be distinguished by whether the available evidence supports a confident decision or warrants further consideration, a distinction conventional classification models are not designed to explicitly represent. We propose a multiscale evidential framework that models cervical cytology along an ordinal disease continuum using clinically grounded soft anchors, representing progression from benign reactive changes to precancerous lesions and malignancy. Three biologically motivated spatial contexts--nuclear morphology, cytoplasmic characteristics, and neighbourhood cellular architecture--are independently encoded using a shared frozen DINOv2 backbone, preserving inter-scale disagreement that early fusion may obscure. Calibrated predictions are transformed into Dempster--Shafer belief masses and fused with reliability discounting, allowing conflicting evidence and insufficient support to remain explicitly uncommitted rather than forcing a decision. The fused belief state drives interpretable routing that separates confidently classified cases from those with irreducible evidential insufficiency, enabling clinically meaningful triage. Across two independent publicly available conventional cytology datasets, RIVA and CRIC Cervix, the framework generalises consistently, with uncertainty strongly associated with diagnostic difficulty: incorrect predictions carry 1.94x-4.13x higher uncertainty than correct ones.
  </details>

- **[Spatially Gated Demographic Steering for Debiasing Multi-Person Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29651)**  `arXiv:2610.29651`  
  _Venkatesh Thirugnana Sambandham, Torsten Schon_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-Image models reproduce societal stereotypes, and existing mitigation applies one demographic correction to the whole scene. In multi-person scene generation this creates a trade-off: global steering improves aggregate balance only by homogenizing the people within an image. Steering-based methods like Embedding Arithmetic drive the intra-image diversity below the unsteered baseline. Text- and latent-projection approaches shift the aggregate skew with only marginal gains in intra-image diversity. We present Spatially Gated Steering (SGS), which steers the demographics of each person independently by extracting per-person masks from the Diffusion Transformer's (DiT) internal attention maps at a critical denoising timestep and applying a distinct demographic vector within each mask for the remaining steps. SGS needs no fine-tuning, inversion or layout specifications, and uses a lightweight segmentor over frozen DiT attention features to localize subjects at early timesteps. On skewed occupational prompts, SGS reaches high intra-image racial and gender diversity while keeping aggregate balance competitive and image quality near baseline, a result we validate on two architecturally distinct diffusion transformers, FLUX.1-Dev and SD3.5-Large. Codebase and model weights are available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[MS2CAST: A Video Anomaly Detection Framework via Multi-Scale Spatial Cross-Attention and Efficient Spatiotemporal Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29649)**  `arXiv:2610.29649`  
  _Ali Safaei, Mohammad Kazemi_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video anomaly detection (VAD) is a challenging task for surveillance systems due to the scarcity and diversity of anomalous events. While semi-supervised VAD approaches have advanced the field, many rely on computationally intensive optical flow networks to capture motion dynamics. Convolutional neural networks (CNNs) are also widely used for spatial encoding, but their effective receptive field is highly local. To address these limitations, we introduce MS2CAST, an end-to-end semi-supervised predictive VAD framework. For spatial encoding, we employ a multi-scale cross-attention encoder that fuses the hierarchical feature representations from a vision transformer (MViTv2-S) and a CNN (ConvNeXt-T) to exploit their distinct receptive fields. To model spatiotemporal dynamics without optical flow estimation, we integrate two interchangeable spatiotemporal modules in the latent space. One module (SwinLSTM) incorporates a Swin Transformer, while the other (VMRNN) integrates Vision Mamba blocks. Our objective function combines L1 loss, Gradient Difference Loss (GDL), and Multi-Scale Structural Similarity Index Measure (MS-SSIM) to ensure pixel-wise accuracy, sharpness, and structural integrity. Evaluations across the UCSD Ped2, CUHK Avenue, and ShanghaiTech datasets demonstrate frame-level Receiver Operating Characteristic (ROC) Area Under the Curve (AUC) scores that are competitive with top-tier techniques while maintaining a peak GPU memory usage of under 1 GB.
  </details>

- **[Joint Semantic-Channel Coding and Modulation for Generative Image Transmission](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29643)**  `arXiv:2610.29643`  
  _Hao Cao, Jingkai Ying, Yinhuan Huang, Zhijin Qin, Yuan Shen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Digital image transmission typically optimizes source coding, channel coding, and modulation separately, limiting how source representations adapt to channel impairments. We propose SemQAM, a joint semantic-channel coding and modulation scheme for generative image transmission converting source to modulated symbols, such as standard QAM symbols. Channel impairments create two challenges: they can corrupt the latent representation at the receiver, and this corruption can compound through autoregressive coding, degrading reconstruction quality. First, SemQAM represents latent features with vector quantization (VQ), maps each VQ index to a QAM symbol, and updates the codeword corresponding to the detected symbol so that channel impairments directly affect codebook learning. Moreover, SemQAM uses the conditional expectation of received codewords to approximate the receiver reconstruction and reduce error propagation during autoregressive coding. SemQAM supports multiple SNRs and CBRs in a unified model, while also supporting conventional source coding through SemQAM-C. Experiments show that (i) under the same LDPC and QAM settings, SemQAM-C outperforms OneDC in LPIPS and DISTS with substantially lower complexity, showing that the unified model retains competitive capability for source coding. (ii) Compared with this separated counterpart, SemQAM reduces channel uses by 18.57% at matched LPIPS and outperforms the compared JSCC methods, demonstrating the benefit of joint coding.
  </details>

- **[OpenEarth-OVS44: A Multimodal Remote Sensing Dataset for Open-Vocabulary Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29642)**  `arXiv:2610.29642`  
  _Yimin Wei, Hongruixuan Chen, Hao Zheng, Hao Liu, Jian Song, Yuting Zhu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In remote sensing, open-vocabulary segmentation is particularly important for flexible and scalable land-cover understanding across diverse regions, as it improves generalization to novel categories without requiring exhaustive pixel-level annotations. Despite its great promise, progress in this area remains largely constrained by existing datasets with limited category coverage and overly coarse taxonomies, and therefore struggles to support fine-grained semantic understanding required in real-world applications such as urban functional zoning, carbon accounting, and post-disaster assessment. To address these challenges, we present OpenEarth-OVS44, a multimodal benchmark with 290,587 expert-verified segments from 2,832 RGB images and 1,341 strictly registered RGB-SAR pairs at sub-meter spatial resolution, covering 40 cities across France, Japan, and the United States under a unified 44-class taxonomy organized into 9 base categories. Three evaluation tracks are provided: an RGB-only fine-grained benchmark, a multimodal track with diverse synthetic cloudy conditions for weather-robust evaluation, and a held-out cross-region track. Extensive experiments on representative open-vocabulary segmentation models demonstrate that training on OpenEarth-OVS44 substantially improves fine-grained segmentation performance over the strongest training-free baseline, with improvements of up to 7.1 mIoU points on unseen categories and 27.6 mIoU points on seen categories. The dataset and code will be released.
  </details>

- **[FogTTA: Online Test-Time Adaptation for Robust Transformer-based Object Detection in Foggy Weather](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29618)**  `arXiv:2610.29618`  
  _Ali Abedi, Q. M. Jonathan Wu, Ning Zhang, Shiqi Tian_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Object detection models for autonomous driving commonly experience substantial performance drops when deployed under adverse weather due to the domain shift between training data and real-world operating conditions. This degradation is especially evident when models trained on clear-weather images encounter foggy environments with reduced visibility and contrast. To address this challenge, we introduce FogTTA, an online test-time adaptation framework designed to improve the robustness of Transformer-based object detectors in fog. Using RF-DETR as the underlying object detector, FogTTA enables real-time adaptation to the streaming target domain without requiring source data or retraining. The framework follows a teacher-student design, where the deployed model serves as the teacher and generates pseudo labels from weakly augmented target inputs. These predictions are subsequently refined through non-maximum suppression and confidence filtering. The student model then learns from strongly augmented target sample using the Varifocal loss to mitigate pseudo-label noise. The teacher is updated via exponential moving averaging to ensure stable and continuous adaptation. Experiments show that FogTTA outperforms prior baselines, delivering improved detection accuracy and stability while maintaining real-time performance.
  </details>

- **[An interpretable deep learning method for early hepatocellular carcinoma diagnosis on multiphase CT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29616)**  `arXiv:2610.29616`  
  _Emma Sarfati, Alexandre Bône, Pietro Gori, Sisi Yang, Marc-Michel Rohé, François Nicolas, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Early diagnosis of hepatocellular carcinoma is critical to preserve access to curative treatment options. The Liver Imaging Reporting and Data System (LI-RADS) provides a standardized framework for non-invasive diagnosis on multiphase computed tomography, based on major visual imaging features. However, these features may be difficult to assess consistently, particularly in small liver lesions. We developed dLI-RADS, an interpretable artificial intelligence method designed to estimate the probability of hepatocellular carcinoma from multiphase computed tomography images of manually segmented liver lesions, while remaining aligned with LI-RADS-related imaging concepts. dLI-RADS combines neural network-derived predictions with handcrafted quantitative imaging measurements reflecting LI-RADS major features and integrates these inputs using logistic regression. In this multicenter retrospective study, patients with multiphase computed tomography and at least one liver lesion were included, while lesions larger than 40 mm were excluded. Histology served as the reference standard for hepatocellular carcinoma, and a composite reference standard was used for non-hepatocellular carcinoma lesions.
