The lesion-level accuracy of dLI-RADS and LI-RADS were compared on the internal evaluation set using 10,000-repetition bootstrap non-inferiority tests, with 5% margins and significance levels. On the external test set, sensitivities were similarly compared. dLI-RADS was non-inferior to LI-RADS for lesion-level accuracy in the internal evaluation set, achieving 80% accuracy compared with 74% for LI-RADS (p = 0.04)...
  </details>

- **[Diff-IrisPAD: Modeling Bona Fide Iris Distributions for Unsupervised Presentation Attack Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29611)**  `arXiv:2610.29611`  
  _Geetanjali Sharma, Harsh Yadav, Tanmay Deshpande, Shubham Ashwani, Aditya Nigam, Raghavendra Ramachandra_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Iris recognition systems are vulnerable to presentation attacks (PAs), including textured contact lenses, print replays, and synthetic irises. Existing PAD methods rely on supervised learning with attack data, limiting generalization to unseen attacks and new sensors. We propose Diff-IrisPAD, an unsupervised framework based on Brownian Bridge Diffusion that models bona fide iris distributions without using attack samples. The method reconstructs clean iris textures from degraded inputs and detects attacks via reconstruction inconsistency using ViT scoring and pixel-level measures. Experiments on a large-scale multi-sensor dataset (125K+ images, NIR and VIS, 8 attack types) show that Diff-IrisPAD achieves an overall ACER of 30.52, significantly outperforming diffusion-based baselines while demonstrating strong generalization to unseen attacks. These results highlight the effectiveness of diffusion-based bona fide modeling for robust and scalable iris PAD.
  </details>

- **[No Frames, Just Frequencies: Bandwidth-Efficient Deployment-Time Adaptation for Smart Glasses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29607)**  `arXiv:2610.29607`  
  _Francesco Corti, Moritz Becker, Olga Saukh_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deep neural networks deployed on smart glasses must adapt to changes in the distribution of incoming data while adhering to strict privacy and device constraints. We present No Frames, Just Frequencies, a bandwidth-efficient source-assisted adaptation framework that characterizes distribution shifts using compact frequency descriptors computed on-device. Rather than transmitting camera frames, the glasses compute and send frequency descriptors to a host device that contains a replica of the model. The host device maps the received frequency descriptor to a distribution shift operator that synthesizes out-of-distribution data and adapts a model replica using a pre-defined adaptation protocol. We evaluate the framework on mixtures of ImageNet-C corruptions using convolution-based (ConvNeXtV2-Femto), self-attention-based (DeiT-Tiny), and hybrid (TinyViT-5M) vision models, on person classification (Visual Wake Words), and on object detection (MS-COCO). Our results reveal that adaptation driven solely by the transmitted descriptors recovers $93-95\%$ of the supervised oracle adaptation gain on all three architectures, $82\%$ on Visual Wake Words and $94$--$98\%$ on MS-COCO. Notably, adaptation quality degrades gracefully as the descriptor resolution decreases from $224 $ to $28$ pixels, consistently across architectures, enabling bandwidth reductions from $213\times$ to $784\times$ across the frequency descriptors.
  </details>

- **[CineEvolve: An Agentic Self-Evolution Framework for Cinematic Multi-Shot Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29590)**  `arXiv:2610.29590`  
  _Jingzhe Zhang, Peixuan Zhang, Genying Li, Bing Ma, Shuchen Weng, Si Li, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Cinematic multi-shot video generation requires both narrative planning and iterative refinement. Existing methods either lack narrative reasoning or rely on feed-forward workflows without policy updates. In this paper, we propose CineEvolve, an agentic self-evolution framework for cinematic multi-shot video generation that unifies a Director and a Critic within a single vision-language policy. Emulating real-world filmmaking that couples iterative direction with critical feedback, we train CineEvolve via alternating reinforcement learning, where the Director surfaces flaws to sharpen the Critic, and the updated Critic guides the Director's targeted revisions. To stabilize co-evolution, we introduce Evolutionary GRPO (E-GRPO), leveraging historical anchoring and revision safeguarding to mitigate advantage inflation. Additionally, we contribute CineTraj, a 40K cinematic trajectory dataset spanning creation, critique, and revision. Extensive experiments demonstrate that CineEvolve outperforms existing approaches. Our code will be available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Unlocking Spatial Grounding in Flow-Matching Models via SNR Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29581)**  `arXiv:2610.29581`  
  _Martin Thißen, Elke Hergenröther_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite the success of foundation models in image synthesis, achieving fine-grained spatial control remains a significant challenge, typically requiring auxiliary structural adapters or complex inference-time steering. While modern vision-language (VLM) text encoders possess the latent capacity to represent spatial coordinates, generative models often fail to leverage this information. We demonstrate that the signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) distribution is a critical determinant of spatial grounding in flow-matching models. Using a novel synthetic benchmark of geometric primitives, we isolate structural mechanics from textural complexity inherent in natural imagery and introduce a rigorous color-aware layout metric to quantify spatial fidelity. We show that training on a centered SNR distribution results in poor layout adherence (25.9%). Instead, we identify high-noise regimes as a "critical learning phase" for spatial grounding. Shifting the training focus toward these stages increases layout adherence to 80.8%, effectively reaching the 83.6% reconstruction ceiling of the underlying variational autoencoder. We find that the necessity for this shift scales with resolution and aspect ratio variance, and that the conservative noise schedules employed by models like FLUX.1 are insufficient for precise layout adherence. Finally, we show that these findings translate to real-world imagery via the COCO dataset (1.0 vs. 57.3 Average Precision), where state-of-the-art layout adherence is unlocked through distribution tuning alone, without architectural modifications.
  </details>

- **[Rethinking Underwater Image Enhancement: Learning through Text-Guided Degradation Curriculum](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29569)**  `arXiv:2610.29569`  
  _Hongri Tian, Wenxue Li, Tian Ye, Sixiang Chen, Lujia Wang, Xianying Wang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing paired clean-degraded datasets have advanced learning-based underwater image enhancement, but they remain either limited in scale or difficult to acquire, and still fail to adequately cover the diverse degradations encountered in real-world underwater environments. Such scarce and insufficient data ultimately restrict the generalization of underwater image enhancement models, highlighting the need for realistic and controllable degradation modeling to better capture real underwater conditions and improve enhancement robustness. To this end, we propose a unified degradation-aware training framework for the underwater image enhancement task. A Text-Conditioned Degradation Augmentation Pipeline uses a text-guided diffusion model to synthesize diverse and semantically consistent degraded samples, providing paired data to support the subsequent curriculum learning strategy. The proposed Progressive Degradation Curriculum organizes training across multiple attributes and difficulty levels to promote balanced learning, and a Transmission-Guided One-Step Diffusion Model leverages this progressive process to better address the wide range of degradation severity and restoration ambiguity in underwater images. Extensive experiments on multiple underwater benchmarks demonstrate that our proposed method achieves superior performance. Our code and models will be released upon the publication of this work.
  </details>

- **[Generalized CycleGRPO for Fine-Grained Multimodal Understanding and Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29552)**  `arXiv:2610.29552`  
  _Xin Zhang, Haochen Wang, Beibei Lin, Zhaoxiang Zhang, Robby T. Tan, Jason Li, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Fine-grained multimodal intelligence requires models to both understand a designated region and localize it from language. Existing methods learn these directions with separate objectives and task-specific annotations. We present Generalized CycleGRPO, the first unified reinforcement learning framework coupling region understanding and localization across spatial, temporal, and spatio-temporal domains. Given a region, a multimodal large language model generates candidate descriptions and then localizes each description to reconstruct the input region. Reconstruction overlap provides a verifiable reward for captioning, while individual localization rewards improve the inverse mapping. A common region-space formulation extends this cycle from image masks and boxes to video intervals and timestamped spatial evidence, without reference captions or an external language-model judge during reinforcement learning. In videos, the cycle encourages descriptions that distinguish a target event from similar recurrences. We introduce DOVE (Distinctive Occurrence-level Video-caption Evaluation), a human-verified contrastive benchmark for this temporal distinctiveness. CycleGRPO improves SAMTok's overall gIoU from 57.5% to 68.2% on GroundingSuite and average gIoU from 71.2% to 78.7% on GRES. For temporal tasks, it achieves 51.9% tIoU on Charades and 33.3% Temporal Evidence Coverage on DOVE, compared with 45.6% and 14.8% for the Qwen3-VL initialization. These results establish region-text cycle consistency as a general self-evaluation principle for multimodal understanding and localization. Models, code, and DOVE are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Hyperspherical Simplex Representations for Backward-Compatible Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29550)**  `arXiv:2610.29550`  
  _Simone Ricci, Niccolò Biondi, Federico Pernici, Alberto Del Bimbo_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Training modern AI models has become increasingly expensive, and updating deployed models can alter their internal feature representations, affecting applications built upon them. In retrieval systems, such updates often require complete gallery re-indexing, a computationally expensive process at scale. Existing backward-compatibility methods enable direct comparison of representations from updated and earlier models, avoiding re-indexing. However, they typically require compatibility-specific training, learned mapping functions, or architectural modifications, and often depend explicitly on the previous model. We propose hyperspherical simplex representations derived from softmax outputs and logits for backward-compatible retrieval across independently trained models. They leverage the simplex structure induced by softmax cross-entropy training with one-hot labels as a common geometric reference across model updates. Since this reference depends on the number of classes, adding new ones expands the simplex, misaligning existing class prototypes with their earlier counterparts. We therefore derive a deterministic linear transformation to restore their alignment. Our theoretical analysis of the formal backward-compatibility definition identifies two geometric conditions under which its inequalities hold in expectation, assuming von Mises--Fisher class distributions with increasing concentration across updates. Aligned regular-simplex representations satisfy these conditions, providing a theoretical basis for the proposed representations under the stated assumptions. Without using additional compatibility-specific losses, learned mappings, or architectural modifications, the proposed representations achieve competitive results on standard compatibility benchmarks, including expanded class sets, more expressive architectures, and independently trained models from public repositories.
  </details>

- **[Melange: A Synthetic Visual Bias Benchmark for Multi-modal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29537)**  `arXiv:2610.29537`  
  _Razieh Rezaei, Ehsaneddin Asgary, Masoud Jalili Sabet_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-modal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have seen broad adoption, but their biases are still far less examined than those of standard LLMs, creating possible social harms when deployed in real-world settings. Prior efforts to extend bias analyses from LLMs into the multi-modal space either cover only a few bias categories or do not ensure that the groups being compared are clearly addressed by visual cues in the images. To overcome these limitations, we introduce Melange, a wide-ranging synthetic visual benchmark covering seven categories for assessing social biases in MLLMs. It is grounded in the widely used language-only BBQ dataset (Bias Benchmark for Question Answering) from BigBench, carefully extending its constructed contexts via controlled generation of visual scenarios so the target attributes appear visually and clearly. To maximize reliability, our generated images pass through two stages of validation: automated screening using visual question answering (VQA) and subsequent manual inspection by human annotators. By systematically evaluating 23 open-source MLLMs across three matched evaluation settings---text-only, image-only, and image-with-context---Melange isolates the fairness effect of the input modality. Switching from text to vision reduces fairness by 0.291 on average, a gap that varies by model family rather than by scale alone. A perception control confirms that this gap is not an artifact of visual comprehension: models identify the depicted attributes at 90--95\% accuracy yet still fail to abstain. When models do commit to a biased answer, polarity-aware stereotype alignment shows they choose the human-stereotypical response roughly three-quarters of the time.
  </details>

- **[Supervision-Adaptive Curriculum and Guarded Anti-Curriculum Learning for Dense Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29519)**  `arXiv:2610.29519`  
  _Chunming He, Dingming Zhang, Fengyang Xiao, Zhiwen Cao, Sina Farsiu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Curriculum learning orders samples by difficulty, but difficulty alone does not establish supervision reliability for dense prediction. Easy samples can expose shortcuts, hard samples can be informative, and noisy or ambiguous samples can mislead training. Building on our conference method CurriSeg, we present PaceShift, a stabilize-then-challenge paradigm combining supervision-adaptive curriculum selection with guarded spectral post-training. A learning-dynamics view motivates separating supervision reliability from temporal update value and applying spectral challenges after stabilization. The first phase estimates reliability from available supervision, augmentation consistency, model disagreement, and prediction certainty. Temporal statistics guide sample weighting, while pixel-uncertainty weights reduce the early influence of uncertain regions. The second phase ranks predictions from multiple spectral views using supervision agreement, robustness, reference consistency, and penalties. A pairwise preference objective measured against the frozen Phase-I reference, together with an anchor, refines the prediction. The framework supports full, weak, semi-supervised, and model-derived supervision. Experiments across context-entangled content segmentation (CECS), broader dense-prediction tasks, and foundation-model and cross-modal adaptations show accuracy gains. Targeted diagnostics indicate improved pooled calibration and corruption robustness in the evaluated settings. PaceShift preserves the deployed architecture and adds no inference computation. Code will be publicly released.
  </details>

- **[Balancing Foundation Models and Trait-Specific Learning for Forehead-Crease Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29495)**  `arXiv:2610.29495`  
  _Geetanjali Sharma, Aryan Jain, Dev Nagaich, Aditya Nigam, Raghavendra Ramachandra_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Biometric recognition often degrades under partial facial vis- ibility, where conventional facial cues are occluded. The forehead region remains consistently visible and contains distinctive crease patterns that can serve as a complementary biometric trait. We present a unified eval- uation of forehead-crease recognition across explainable zero-shot rea- soning, domain-adaptive learning, and trait-specific modeling. FIRE en- ables interpretable zero-shot verification using multimodal large language models, while pretrained face-recognition models are adapted to the fore- head domain through margin-based learning. Under open-set evaluation, the best zero-shot model GPT-4o+FIRE achieves 18.22% EER, while Edge-Forehead achieves 8.30% EER and 54.01% TMR@0.1% FMR. The results show that although MLLMs offer interpretable zero-shot verifi- cation, trait-specific learning remains more effective for robust forehead- crease biometric recognition.
  </details>

- **[BlazeVGGT: 13x Faster VGGT at 2,000 Frames via Camera Token without Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29454)**  `arXiv:2610.29454`  
  _Shujun Yao, Junhui Lin, Congying Han, Tiande Guo, Ziwen Liu, Bonan Li_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Feed-forward visual geometry models, exemplified by VGGT, have demonstrated strong multi-view reconstruction capabilities through dense global attention. However, the resulting all-to-all cross-view computation scales quadratically with the number of input views, making long-sequence inference particularly expensive due to substantial frame-level redundancy among highly overlapping observations. We uncover a topology-first, geometry-later evolution in VGGT camera tokens: shallow-layer tokens reliably capture local view neighborhoods, whereas deeper-layer tokens progressively encode globally organized inter-view geometry. Motivated by this observation, we introduce BlazeVGGT, a training-free framework for structure-aware frame-level key--value sparsification. At each global-attention block, BlazeVGGT constructs a view-level similarity kernel from camera-token relations and employs greedy determinantal point process inference to select a compact set of mutually complementary views. The selected views constitute the key--value context, while all input frames are retained as queries. This asymmetric attention design preserves dense per-frame predictions while eliminating redundant cross-view computation, allowing our method to be directly integrated into pretrained VGGT as a plug-and-play module without parameter updates. Its advantages become increasingly pronounced with sequence length: at 2,000 frames, BlazeVGGT achieves approximately 13$\times$ faster inference than VGGT while reducing the Chamfer Distance from 0.41 to 0.32. Extensive experiments demonstrate a favorable accuracy--efficiency trade-off in point-cloud reconstruction and camera-pose estimation, compatibility with existing VGGT accelerators such as FastVGGT and LiteVGGT, and transferability to VGGT-based models including StreamVGGT and PAGE-4D. Codes are publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Shortcut Learning: Hierarchical Spatial Prototype Regularization for Face Forgery Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29442)**  `arXiv:2610.29442`  
  _Chao Shuai, Shaojing Fan, Kun Pan, Jinwu Hu, Weichen Lian, Ziqi Yang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent advances in generative models have enabled highly realistic face forgeries, yet generalizing deepfake detectors to unseen manipulation pipelines remains a major challenge. One critical yet underexplored issue is that the learned forensic representation itself may fail to remain stable across different manipulation sources. In this paper, we provide new evidence that naively fine-tuned vision models suffer from severe \textit{forensic representation collapse}: their extracted cues are often entangled with unstable, dataset-specific artifacts, resulting in structurally fragile representations and poor cross-domain generalization. Leveraging this insight, we propose \textbf{H}ierarchical \textbf{S}patial \textbf{P}rototype (\textbf{HSP}) regularization, which explicitly models the forgery manifold by learning a structured prototype subspace over spatial features. We further introduce prototype orthogonality and second-order assignment balance, which jointly preserve the geometric independence of latent forgery factors and promote high-effective-rank utilization of the forensic representation, thereby reducing reliance on dataset-specific shortcuts. Empirical results demonstrate that our method substantially improves generalizability, yielding average frame-level AUC gains of 5.6\% and 5.3\% in the cross-dataset and cross-manipulation settings, respectively. Moreover, when used as a plug-in module, HSP boosts the average video-level AUC of existing state-of-the-art detectors to 95.3\% (+1.8\%). These results demonstrate the practical value of HSP as an easy-to-adopt regularization module for enhancing deepfake detectors.
  </details>

- **[Sparsity Should Be ALLOTted by Output: Training-Free Allocation of Block-Sparse Attention for Video Diffusion Transformers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29441)**  `arXiv:2610.29441`  
  _Tianze Xia, Xiaoling Zhou, Ruya Lan, Yiqiao Liao, Chengru Song, Shiming Xiang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Full 3D attention is costly in video Diffusion Transformers. Block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing only selected attention blocks. However, existing methods either overlook the potential to optimize sparsity allocation or rely on heuristic rules or local metrics to guide it. In this work, we observe that sparsifying attention in different layers and at different denoising steps affects final-video fidelity to substantially different degrees. Yet the identities of the most sensitive layer-step pairs remain nearly unchanged across inputs. Building on these findings, we propose Allocating Layer-step Latency by Output Tests (ALLOT), a training-free sparsity allocation method. Treating each layer-step pair as a cell, ALLOT estimates each cell's contribution to final-video error through (i) cell probing and (ii) trajectory calibration, then jointly optimizes cell densities under a measured-latency budget. It preserves existing selection strategies and kernels, replacing only the allocation component. At equal or shorter wall-clock time, ALLOT outperforms every compared allocation strategy with XAttention, with an 11.8 dB PSNR gain on HunyuanVideo. Extensive experiments across four additional published pipelines on Wan2.1, Wan2.2, and HunyuanVideo show that ALLOT outperforms or ties the default in all nine settings.
  </details>

- **[Look Beyond the Question: Preserve-Focus-Fuse for Whole-Slide Image Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29431)**  `arXiv:2610.29431`  
  _Xiangyu Li, Jincheng Li, Tianle Du, Yanheng Li, Haoxuan Che, Yuxin Sun, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  A question about a whole-slide image rarely names every morphological finding needed to answer it. Question-guided patch selection can therefore overlook supporting morphology, while slide-level compression can dilute focal evidence. Inspired by how pathologists combine broad slide review, question-directed examination, and integration of their findings, we introduce BEYONDPATH, a Preserve-Focus-Fuse framework for whole-slide image question answering. Preserve learns a question-agnostic Morphology Bank through answer supervision across multiple questions about each training slide. Focus retrieves questionspecific evidence from all foreground patches, including regions outside the Bank. Fuse integrates the preserved morphology at the retrieved evidence positions through Morphology-on-Visual-Evidence Fusion, producing a fixed 96-token visual prefix. On WSI-Bench, BEYONDPATH achieves an Avg-9 of 0.769 and leads all eight reported report-generation metrics using the same checkpoint. It also achieves 0.639 zero-shot overall accuracy on SlideBench-BCNB. Controlled ablations show complementary benefits from morphology preservation and question-directed retrieval, and support cross-question supervision and fusion at the evidence positions. These results support looking beyond the question's explicit cues: preserving broader slide morphology while retaining the ability to seek evidence for each question.
  </details>

- **[LLMSeg: Training-Free Text-Prompted Image Segmentation with B-Splines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29409)**  `arXiv:2610.29409`  
  _Abdullah Hamdi_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Promptable segmentation models such as SAM 3 achieve remarkable open-vocabulary performance but require dedicated architectures, large-scale annotation engines, and task-specific training. We ask a different question: can a general-purpose multimodal large language model (MLLM) segment images directly, without an added segmentation head, mask decoder, or training? We present LLMSeg, a training-free pipeline that turns a vision-capable LLM into a text-prompted image segmenter. LLMSeg decomposes segmentation into three LLM-driven stages. First, detect localizes all instances matching a free-form text query and describes their coarse morphology. Next, segment traces each instance using closed periodic B-splines confined to its detected box. These splines define an outer boundary and optional hole loops. Finally, refine iteratively proposes focus boxes around residual errors and retraces confined splines. The process ends when the mask stabilizes or the pass budget is exhausted. The output is a binary mask with inspectable intermediate geometry. We evaluate LLMSeg across ten referring and reasoning segmentation splits (ReasonSeg and RefCOCO/+/g). We also evaluate open-vocabulary instance segmentation (LVIS, COCO, and SA-Co), semantic segmentation, and zero-shot detection (ODinW13 and RF-100VL). With an off-the-shelf Gemini-3.7-Flash backend, LLMSeg is competitive with and often surpasses the specialist SAM 3 model on identical items. It reaches 71.5 cIoU on ReasonSeg val without additional segmentation-specific training. Backend model updates can improve accuracy without retraining the pipeline. The code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[LiveAct: Towards Real-Time Multi-Agent World Models with Heterogeneous Action Interfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29408)**  `arXiv:2610.29408`  
  _Xinyu Wang, Huafeng Shi, Yue Ma, Zijie Meng, Teng Xi, Yuzhuo Chen, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Interactive video world models predict how an environment evolves under user actions, but most existing systems assume one controlled agent and one task-specific action interface. We study multi-agent video world modeling, where one model generates a joint visual future for multiple controlled agents while interpreting heterogeneous controls ranging from binary game buttons to continuous robot commands. This task presents two complementary challenges. First, every agent-indexed action stream must control its intended visual entity independently. Second, the model must adapt the meaning of each control variable across different tasks, where the same tensor dimension may denote an attack, steering command, or robot command. We introduce LiveAct, centered on a Heterogeneous Action Interface (HAI) whose two components mirror this decomposition. To support independent control, Agent-Aware Spatial Modulation uses language-driven spatial gates and agent-specific attention to bind each action stream to its intended visual agent. To accommodate heterogeneous controls, Schema-Guided Action Adaptation describes each control variable in language and modulates its semantic token with the synchronized numerical value. We further transfer the complete interface to a blockwise causal generator through teacher forcing and rollout-aware distribution matching, enabling efficient streaming generation while retaining agent-specific control and action semantics. We instantiate the formulation on six multi-agent sources spanning games and multi-robot manipulation, and evaluate visual quality, action following, agent attribution, and latency. Across games and robot manipulation, LiveAct reaches 0.898 and 0.942 agent-attribution accuracy without agent locations or identity crops, and the causal generator attains 17.70 FPS at $512\times 448$.
  </details>

- **[VideoGen Model 1: Prompt Expansion Versus Formatting in Audiovisual Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29407)**  `arXiv:2610.29407`  
  _David Grossman, Anton Koenig_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Creative prompt expansion improved overall audiovisual quality scores over raw prompts in a controlled pilot of VideoGen Model 1, our post-trained version of MiniMax H3. We compared the raw, reformatted, and expanded versions of 30 prompts, holding the generator, seed, and sampling settings fixed. Each clip was rated separately on five dimensions. Their mean formed an overall score on a 1-10 scale. Compared with formatting alone, overall scores increased by 0.63 points (95% interval: [+0.11, +1.15]) under VA-Judger and 0.15 points (95% interval: [-0.03, +0.35]) under Gemini 3.8 Flash, whose overall change remained uncertain. Prompt-alignment scores increased by 0.97 points under VA-Judger and 0.50 under Gemini. VA-Judger also showed higher audio quality; visual-quality ratings changed little under either judge.
  </details>

- **[FM-Occ: Feature-Diverse Upsampling and Memory-Aware Decoding for 3D Occupancy Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29388)**  `arXiv:2610.29388`  
  _Yujia Zhai, Guoming Huang, Zhuanghuai Wang, Bangquan Xie_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D semantic occupancy prediction estimates voxel-wise occupancy and semantic labels throughout a scene. Our experiments reveal that conventional sparse transposed-convolution upsampling yields poorly differentiated voxel responses and that successive decoder layers discard previously correct semantic predictions. To address these issues, we present Feature-Diverse Upsampling and Memory-Aware Decoding for 3D Occupancy Prediction (FM-Occ), comprising two components. First, 3D Sparse Subvoxel Shuffle (3S) mitigates feature homogenization by constructing candidate child features in the channel dimension, jointly transforming and normalizing them, and then rearranging them onto a finer sparse grid with explicit feature-coordinate correspondence. Second, Entropy-Regulated Layer-wise Memory (ER-LM) retains semantic information across decoder layers by maintaining a layer-wise memory for each query through gated recurrent updates and using prediction entropy to regulate the historical information propagated to the next layer. During training, a class-weighted entropy loss Lcwe provides label-guided regularization for matched semantic queries. On SemanticKITTI, 3S increases the spatial coefficient of variation of normalized voxel-wise root-mean-square (RMS) responses from 0.41% to 4.03%, while ER-LM reduces semantic degradation and improves error correction across decoder layers. The combined FM-Occ improves mIoU from 12.16% to 12.73% with negligible memory overhead. ER-LM further improves mIoU in the BEV-based DISC framework from 16.78% to 17.17%, supporting its applicability across 3D voxel-based and BEV-based architectures.
  </details>

- **[AdvFidelity: Unifying Preference Alignment and Few-Step Image Generation within Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29380)**  `arXiv:2610.29380`  
  _Sicong Kang, Fangao Zeng, Yuhan Li, Hao Zhou, Mengfei Xu, Wenxiang Shang, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Practical image generation requires both human preference alignment and few-step efficiency, yet combining reinforcement learning (RL) with distillation is often multi-stage and may erode RL gains We observe that few-step RL progressively improves structural coherence, even with specialized rewards, but leaves low-level artifacts that these rewards may fail to penalize. To address this gap, we propose AdvFidelity, which integrates artifact-sensitive fidelity supervision into RL through an online adversarial reward. This enables joint reward alignment and high-fidelity few-step generation within a single RL stage, without distribution matching distillation (DMD) or other explicit distillation objectives. We train a discriminator to rank high-quality references (positives) above content-matched reconstructions (negatives) produced by the current generator. Reconstructing moderately noised references largely preserves style and scene layout while exposing low-level degradations. This encourages the discriminator to assess artifacts rather than content differences. With negatives reconstructed online, alternating updates adapt fidelity supervision to the generator's evolving failure modes. References can come from existing target-domain data or full-step model samples. Built on LeapAlign, AdvFidelity backpropagates gradients from both rewards through the few-step sampling trajectories used at inference. Across two model backbones and three task settings, AdvFidelity matches full-step LeapAlign in alignment, task performance, and overall generation quality and remains highly competitive with other evaluated full-step RL methods, using only 4 or 8 steps without CFG.
  </details>

- **[Seeing through Denoising: Frequency-guided One-step Denoising for Infrared Small Target Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29361)**  `arXiv:2610.29361`  
  _Xuelin Qian, Zixuan Wang, Chang Xu, Dingwen Zhang, Junwei Han_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Infrared small target detection (IRSTD) is a challenging task due to the extremely small size of targets, low signal-to-noise ratios, and heavy background clutter in infrared imagery. While the progress of deep learning has advanced this field, existing methods primarily adopt discriminative paradigms that directly map images to target masks, overlooking the potential of generative modeling. In this paper, we propose DiffIRSTD, a novel generative framework that reformulates infrared small target detection as a background reconstruction problem. Instead of explicitly predicting target regions, we reinterpret small targets as noise-like signals and leverage a diffusion-based model to recover a clean, target-free background from a perturbed input image. The residual between the reconstructed background and the original image is then exploited to highlight candidate target regions, providing explicit cues for subsequent detection. To address the computational cost typically associated with diffusion sampling, we introduce a one-step denoising strategy that enables efficient background reconstruction while maintaining practical inference speed. Furthermore, we present a frequency-guided perception loss to preserve high-frequency background details and focus more on the reconstruction quality of target regions. More importantly, our DiffIRSTD operates as a plug-and-play framework, compatible with existing IRSTD models without increasing the computational complexity of detectors. Extensive experiments on IRSTD-1K, NUAA-SIRST, and NUDT-SIRST datasets demonstrate the superiority of our method, achieving state-of-the-art performance across three benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[Reading the Mask: Descriptor-Grounded BI-RADS Rationale Generation for Breast MRI Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29358)**  `arXiv:2610.29358`  
  _Hrishikesh Singh, Aarti Sharma, Divyanshu Kumar, Millie Pant_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deep learning based models accurately delineate tumours on dynamic contrast-enhanced MRI (DCE-MRI), but segmentation masks alone do not provide the rationales or reasoning used by radiologists to assess the lesion suspicion. This limits the explainability as well the clinical integration. We investigate whether clinically relevant descriptors can instead be derived from the segmentation masks and their dynamic contrast-enhancement series. We have used Breast Imaging Reporting and Data System (BI-RADS) guidelines as a structured framework. Using these guidelines we designed a BI-RADS decision tree with eight quantitative measurements using radiomics, together with the lesion size and component count, that determine the descriptors at eight nodes. Using these descriptors we generate natural-language rationale using a large language model. The model was constrained to express only the derived records from the decision tree. After applying this framework to the expert and automatic masks of each of the 1,506 cases in the MAMA-MIA dataset, we generated a reproducible corpus of 3,012 BI-RADS grounded rationales. We obtained a faithfulness score of 99.98% and a counterfactual accuracy of 99.3%, indicating that the generated rationales faithfully verbalises the measured descriptors and correctly reflects changes in the underlying record. Agreement between the descriptors from expert and automatic masks falls from 0.832 to 0.549 as mask quality falls from Good to Missed. The generated rationales are intended as draft representations for radiologist review and final sign-off rather than autonomous clinical reports.
  </details>

- **[OpenMAP-HIE: Pathology Informed Score-based Diffusion Model for Unsupervised Neonatal Hypoxic-Ischemic Encephalopathy Lesion Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29322)**  `arXiv:2610.29322`  
  _Pengyu Kan, Craig Jones, Kenichi Oishi_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Accurate localization of neonatal hypoxic--ischemic encephalopathy (HIE) lesions on apparent diffusion coefficient (ADC) images is clinically important but is limited by scarce expert annotations and substantial variation in lesion appearance. We propose OpenMAP-HIE, a pathology-informed score-based diffusion model for unsupervised HIE lesion localization without voxel-wise lesion supervision. A denoising diffusion model learns the healthy neonatal ADC distribution, while a ControlNet-based conditional branch preserves patient-specific anatomy. At inference, anomaly evidence is derived directly from differences between conditional and unconditioned noise predictions at independently evaluated diffusion noise levels, avoiding dependence on a final pseudo-healthy reconstruction or sequential reverse-diffusion chain. Based on the characteristic ADC reduction associated with acute HIE, only discrepancies consistent with the expected pathological direction are retained and aggregated across noise levels using a geometric mean. Spatial mean filtering further suppresses isolated responses. The framework was evaluated on the BONBID-HIE dataset. The ADC-only method achieved a Dice score of $0.283\pm0.244$, average precision of $0.505$, Hausdorff distance of $25.45\pm15.63$ mm, mean average surface distance of $9.40\pm8.49$ mm, and normalized surface distance of $0.355\pm0.236$, providing the best overlap and surface-distance performance among the evaluated unsupervised methods and Dice performance comparable to supervised ADC-only models. Optional population-derived ADC $Z$-map refinement further increased Dice to $0.602\pm0.269$. These findings support pathology-informed score discrepancies as a useful signal for neonatal HIE lesion localization, while highlighting remaining challenges in detecting small and low-contrast lesions.
  </details>

- **[Latent space configuration for neural network finetuning, fast ranking, and embedding dimensionality reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29295)**  `arXiv:2610.29295`  
  _Nikita Gabdullin, Anton Raskovalov_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Finding K most similar elements for a query in a large gallery of neural network (NN) embeddings is essential in various areas of computer science and industrial applications of artificial intelligence. This paper proposes search algorithms for structured latent spaces (LS) of NNs which allow to speed up top K item retrieval owing to O(1) complexity small candidate gallery subset search. ArcFace-pretrained IResNet50 models are finetuned using Latent Space Configuration (LSC) methodology to obtain new distribution of embeddings with desired geometric properties. Applying the proposed algorithms to embeddings obtained from finetuned models leads to several times speed-up over baseline exhaustive search. Specifically, we report up to 3 times speed-up on GPU and 40% speed-up on CPU compared to the baseline method GPU speed. These results are obtained for the original and reduced numbers of LS dimensions. The results show that LS dimensionality reduction allows to further accelerate the search and even improve rank-N metrics.
  </details>

- **[Certified Abstention for Provably-Unidentifiable Index-Phase Recovery in Image Registration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29272)**  `arXiv:2610.29272`  
  _Dinh Tuan Nguyen, Trong Nhan Nguyen, Vu Nguyen Minh Hung, Tien Minh Khoi Nguyen, Jaeyeop Choi, Sudip Mondal, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image registration underpins measurement across scanning, camera, and microscopy imaging, yet a fundamental question is rarely posed: when is the sample-to-position map recoverable at all, and when does any estimator that reports a position necessarily fabricate one? We show that in reference-perturbed sampled imaging, where samples are localized through a perturbed reference, position fidelity (where samples land) and phase fidelity (which reference index becomes each block's origin) are logically independent: no policy acting on position information alone can guarantee phase fidelity. We derive a tight resolution threshold separating recoverable from provably-unidentifiable registration, and a recovery framework that certifies identifiable regions--recovering them exactly--while abstaining on provably-unidentifiable regions rather than hallucinating structure. On a deployed scanning system we measure a direction-bimodal registration offset consistent with the theory. Against classical and learned restoration baselines, certificate-free offset estimators fabricate structure on the unidentifiable regime, whereas the certified method abstains rather than fabricating. Finally, the certified-abstention mechanism carries across scanning, rolling-shutter, and cryo-electron-microscopy imaging--declining rather than fabricating on the regime it flags--though how completely it abstains and how tightly it bounds kept-region risk stay modality-specific (at extreme SNR the cryo-EM certificate abstains only partially): a structural-versus-statistical boundary in registration reliability.
  </details>

- **[Clinical radar heart sounds with echocardiographic phenotypes of ventricular dysfunction and valvular disease](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29267)**  `arXiv:2610.29267`  
  _Jinye Li, Qingchao Chen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Radar heart sounds capture cardiac mechanical activity without contact, while echocardiography characterizes ventricular and valvular function. Paired measurements connect these signals to structural heart disease, yet such data remain sparsely represented in public resources. Here we present 537 radar heart-sound recordings paired with one echocardiographic report for each of 162 patients. The cohort covers three overlapping phenotypes: left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF) below 50%, regional wall-motion abnormality and at least moderate valvular regurgitation, present in 38, 43 and 39 patients, respectively. Original complex signals, processed radar heart sounds and temporal quality segmentation support signal-to-phenotype analysis. Technical validation retains 95.93% of signal duration and confirms reproducible waveform reconstruction. Patient-level five-fold evaluation achieves a mean outer-test AUROC of $0.770\pm0.138$ for reduced LVEF, identifying mechanical information associated with ventricular systolic dysfunction. This resource supports research linking non-contact cardiac mechanical signals to echocardiographic structural and functional phenotypes.
  </details>

- **[CRAB: Cuboid-based Representation for As-built BIM from Point Clouds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29257)**  `arXiv:2610.29257`  
  _Haoran Xiong, Jing Wang, Shanshan Pan, Zihao Yan, Minglun Gong, Hui Huang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As-built BIM reconstruction from point clouds demands semantic, volumetric, parametric, and editable building components, beyond 2D layouts or planar abstractions. Yet existing scan-to-BIM pipelines often rely on handcrafted rules, heuristic optimization, or manual correction, while learning-based methods fall short of recovering wall thickness, opening geometry, and component-level BIM structure. We introduce CRAB, a BIM-ready cuboid reconstruction framework that encodes walls, doors, windows, beams, and columns as compact architectural cuboids with IFC-compatible semantics and parameters. CRAB bridges point clouds and editable BIM by combining ProposalNet, which predicts coarse component cuboids, with AssemblyNet, a relation-aware Flow Matching model that refines geometry, predicts structural relationships, and enables automatic structural correction and human-guided editing. To address limited cuboid-level supervision, we extend Structured3D with component-wise cuboid annotations and construct cuboid-based procedural scenes. Experiments show CRAB surpasses floor plan and plane-based alternatives in component completeness, geometric accuracy, and generalization. For interior wall thickness estimation, CRAB achieves a mean error of 2.80 cm, with 79.50% of matched walls within 3 cm. The project page is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[ER-Chord: Spatially Consistent One-Step Image Editing via Equal-Energy Reliability Redistribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29248)**  `arXiv:2610.29248`  
  _Peize Li, Hanzhen Zhao, Xingguo Xu, Yilong Fan, Zhen Tao, Bo Yin, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  One-step generative models enable fast text-guided image editing, yet they often introduce unintended changes to regions that should remain unchanged. Temporal aggregation stabilizes editing fields across noise levels, but leaves their spatial allocation insensitive to local variations in reliability. Consequently, temporal stability alone does not ensure spatially consistent editing. To address this lim- itation, we propose ER-Chord, a training-free and inversion-free framework for reliability-guided spatial redistribution. Our key insight is that agreement between editing fields at neighboring noise levels provides a local reliability signal for allocating editing strength. We formulate this allocation as an equal-energy op- timization that redistributes control energy according to local reliability while preserving the original editing directions and total control energy. This formu- lation admits a unique global solution that can be efficiently computed using existing field evaluations, requiring no additional denoiser calls. Experiments on two benchmarks, PIE-Bench and TEdBench, demonstrate improvements over state-of-the-art one-step editing baselines, achieving superior source preservation alongside consistent gains across multiple generativethis http URLproject page is atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Encoded but Unused: Bounding the Sources of VLM Errors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29241)**  `arXiv:2610.29241`  
  _Anat Veikherman, Mor Ventura, Nitay Calderon, Lihi Zelnik-Manor, Roi Reichart_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In this paper, we investigate why vision-language models (VLMs) produce errors by decomposing them into two sources: visual-representation failures, in which the visual evidence required for the correct answer is not encoded, and visual-utilization failures, in which this evidence is encoded but not successfully used. We propose to estimate the visual-utilization failure rate using lightweight probes and derive theoretical bounds on the true rate that account for probe imperfections. We introduce VELens (Visual Evidence Lens), a rank-based probe that traces visual evidence for both a target object and its ground-truth attribute, such as color, material, or shape, across VLM layers. We evaluate VELens on over 6,000 images using LLaVA-1.6-7B and Qwen2.5-VL-7B. Our method achieves ROC-AUC scores of 0.95 and 0.91, respectively, and outperforms alternative probes in both low-resource and unseen-attribute settings. Using it to diagnose errors, we estimate that visual-utilization failures account for 84.4% of LLaVA errors and 70.0% of Qwen errors, with conservative lower bounds of 77.5% and 61.0%, respectively. Overall, our study identifies visual utilization as the dominant bottleneck in VLMs and suggests that a central opportunity for improvement lies in developing better mechanisms to locate, route, and use the visual information they already encode.
  </details>

- **[Think-Capture: Active 3D Geometry Acquisition for Spatial Reasoning in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29232)**  `arXiv:2610.29232`  
  _Junjie Wang, Zhihao Yuan, Yunhan Tang, Sheng Xu, Jiaqi Mao, Xiaoyu Ye, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-Language Models (VLMs) excel at 2D recognition but often fail to maintain consistent 3D relations across sparse viewpoints, leading to spatial hallucinations under occlusion and perspective changes. Existing methods commonly fuse all frames with dense geometry, incurring substantial computation and context overhead while encouraging blind reliance on imperfect estimators. We present Think-Capture, a query-conditioned framework that first identifies task-critical objects and then acquires their geometry on demand. A 3D toolkit lifts zero-shot masks into a common coordinate frame, and a Symbolic 3D State Abstraction compresses object and camera states into structured text. Hierarchical Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) trains the VLM on cases in which geometry either helps or misleads, teaching it when to use tool evidence and when to fall back on visual cues. Think-Capture-4B reaches 54.4% on MindCube-Tiny (+21.5 points over its backbone), 39.1% on the MMSI-Bench positional subset (+7.9), and 42.8% on ViewSpatial (+5.2). It also remains robust under coordinate and mask perturbations. The full stack fits one 24 GB GPU and serves as an asynchronous high-level reasoner on an Agibot G1. Our code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[DisasterRoute: Evidence-Guided Dual-System Routing for Visual Disaster Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29231)**  `arXiv:2610.29231`  
  _Tan Zhang, Xing Deng, Lu Zhang, Xiaofeng Zhu, Ping Hu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual question answering from low-altitude UAV imagery must deliver timely and accurate judgments to support disaster response under limited edge computing resources. Routine questions may be answered through direct decisions, whereas more challenging questions may benefit from step-by-step reasoning. However, existing research primarily focuses on improving models' visual understanding and generative reasoning capabilities, with limited exploration of efficient direct decision-making and its adaptive coordination with generative reasoning. We develop Disaster-S1 based on a vision-language model adapted to the disaster domain. Trained for option discrimination, it outputs a probability distribution over answer options in a single multimodal forward pass for fast decision-making. We further propose DisasterRoute, a dual-system routing framework that combines prediction confidence with disaster evidence to decide whether to accept the initial answer or invoke the disaster reasoning model DisasterVL for review. By allocating inference computation on demand, the framework retains the efficiency of direct decision-making and improves overall question-answering accuracy through selective review. Experiments on DisasterBench show that Disaster-S1 achieves superior accuracy with low latency, while DisasterRoute further improves accuracy through selective review at substantially lower latency than full reasoning.
  </details>

- **[Cache Locally, Recompute Selectively for Streaming Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29229)**  `arXiv:2610.29229`  
  _Junhyeok Lee, Sangyoon Lee, Jaeho Lee_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Streaming video understanding (SVU) requires video-LLMs to process incoming video continuously while answering user queries with low latency. Recent methods reduce query-time cost by prefilling frames into KV caches during idle time and reusing the cached KVs at query time. To bound per-frame computation over unbounded video streams, these methods restrict the context available during prefill. This creates a fundamental context mismatch: the restricted context may omit information needed to properly contextualize video tokens, yet, still contain information irrelevant to the eventual query, which is unknown during prefill. We propose FrameBlend, a simple yet effective plug-and-play method to address this mismatch through selective KV recomputation, without requiring any additional training. At query time, FrameBlend re-prefills selected video tokens using query-relevant context and replaces their cached KVs with recomputed ones. Crucially, this recomputation enables shorter attention windows during idle-time prefill, substantially reducing prefill cost while improving accuracy. Experiments across three SVU frameworks and five streaming and offline benchmarks demonstrate accuracy gains of up to 3.9 pp. On RVS-Ego, FrameBlend reduces per-frame prefill FLOPs by over 20% and total processing time by 14-59% for retrieval-based frameworks, including the cost of query-time recomputation. These results demonstrate that a simple change in how contextualization is distributed between idle time and query time can improve the accuracy-efficiency trade-off for SVU.
  </details>

- **[DetM-Tracker: Deterministic and Memory-Efficient VIS for Autonomous Industrial Inspection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29212)**  `arXiv:2610.29212`  
  _You-Rui Lin, Jen-Chieh Wu, Tzu-Ti Wei, Jen-Jee Chen, Yu-Chee Tseng_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Autonomous industrial inspection requires strictly deterministic real-time inference under tight memory and computational constraints. However, existing Video Instance Segmentation (VIS) models suffer from prohibitive scaling: dense temporal memory grows with spatial resolution and sequence length, while fixed-capacity decoders fail to exploit the inherent sparsity of industrial scenes, creating a practical memory wall on resource-constrained edge platforms. We present DetM-Tracker, a streamlined VIS framework for autonomous industrial inspection, where target objects often exhibit highly similar appearance under constrained "micro" fields of view. DetM-Tracker eliminates architectural redundancy through three synergistic components: (1) Compact Scalar Memory, which condenses high-dimensional instance features into four-scalar descriptors, reducing temporal memory complexity from $O(T \cdot N \cdot R)$ to $O(T \cdot \hat{N})$. This mechanism effectively compresses frame-level representations into a bank of active instances, where $R$ denotes resolution, $N$ and $\hat{N}$ represent global and window-specific instance counts, respectively and $T$ the temporal window size; and (2) Dynamic Instance Channels, which adaptively allocate query capacity according to the actual number of visible objects; and (3) Entropy-Gated Skip Connection, which concentrates computational budget on high-uncertainty spatial regions to refine object boundaries in a single forward pass without auxiliary refinement heads. Our approach effectively bridges the gap between modern VIS models and the practical demands of industrial edge deployment, reducing inference latency by approximately 20$\times$ and VRAM consumption by 6$\times$ compared to state-of-the-art dense-feature baselines.
  </details>

- **[NeuralSRender: G-Buffer Guided Video Diffusion for Sim-to-Real Rendering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29200)**  `arXiv:2610.29200`  
  _Yipeng Lu, Youqi Liao, Zhenyu Wang, Yuning Peng, Zhen Dong_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The scarcity of high-quality annotated visual data remains a critical bottleneck for autonomous-driving perception. Simulators provide training data at scale, but the limited realism of synthetic videos compromises perception performance in real-world applications. To address this limitation, we propose NeuralSRender, a sim-to-real video translation framework that enhances the photorealism of synthetic videos while maintaining geometric consistency with the source scenes. Built on a pretrained video diffusion transformer (DiT), NeuralSRender uses a lightweight projector to inject geometric and material priors from G-buffers, and LoRA enables parameter-efficient adaptation. The model is trained in two stages: it first learns G-buffer conditioning from synthetic videos with accurate G-buffers, then adapts to real-world appearance using real videos with estimated G-buffers. During the second stage, an additional LoRA module is trained while the projector and first-stage LoRA remain frozen to reduce interference from estimation noise. NeuralSRender introduces only 1.45\% parameter overhead and can be trained on consumer-grade GPUs with 24 GB of memory. To support training and evaluation, we introduce GenReal, a large-scale driving video dataset comprising synthetic and real-world videos with corresponding G-buffers. Experiments demonstrate improved photorealism with competitive geometric consistency. Training perception models on our translated videos instead of raw simulator renders improves detection $\mathrm{AP}_{50}$ on nuImages from 35.1 to 40.6 and segmentation mIoU on Cityscapes from 32.4 to 40.8. Our code, data, and model weights are available at:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[ROCOCO: Robust Outline Completion of Occluded Curvilinear Objects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29177)**  `arXiv:2610.29177`  
  _Ken Zheng, Jaimyn Drake_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We propose a learning-based generative model for completing curvilinear features under occlusion by environmental obstacles. To this end, we adopt a flow matching-based approach that supports the inpainting of occluded regions with the appropriate curvilinear features. To eliminate the reliance on privileged information about the occlusion a priori, we also train a UNet-based mask prediction model, which can identify regions for inpainting given only the occluded observation. The components of our approach are validated via experiments using procedurally-generated test cases, and the complete pipeline is validated using large public datasets for curvilinear objects. Against off-the-shelf inpainting baselines, ROCOCO is the strongest method by a wide margin when no occlusion mask is given, and is competitive with mask-supervised inpainting when one is, achieving best on full-image error and second only to LaMa on the occluded region, while using a completer of $7.7$M parameters against LaMa's $51$M and Stable Diffusion's $860$M.
  </details>

- **[LocalAgent: Collaborative Agentic Verification for Fine-Grained Instance-Level Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29174)**  `arXiv:2610.29174`  
  _Jiachen Guo, Xinshan Zhu, Boyun Wang, Yanyan Liang, Jun Wan, Feng Lin, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made rapid progress in general visual understanding, yet they still struggle with fine-grained instance-level consistency verification, where the goal is to determine whether two images depict the exact same physical object under varying viewpoints, illumination, and backgrounds. This task requires both global semantic perception and precise examination of subtle local evidence, since semantically similar objects may differ only in small suspicious regions. To address this challenge, we propose LocalAgent, a collaborative agentic framework that integrates the complementary strengths of a generalist MLLM and lightweight specialist verifiers. The MLLM first reasons globally to identify suspicious local regions, which are then delegated to a Local Evidence Verifier (LEV) toolkit for fine-grained appearance and geometric matching. LEV comprises Local Appearance Consistency (LAC), which estimates appearance similarity under illumination and color changes, and Local Geometric Correspondence (LGC), which estimates spatial correspondence under viewpoint changes. LEV returns structured scalar evidence that the MLLM integrates for the final decision. We further introduce an auxiliary-capped reinforcement fine-tuning (RFT) strategy, in which answer correctness dominates auxiliary tool-use and format signals, enabling the policy to learn when verification is necessary. Experiments show that LocalAgent improves mean accuracy by 4.9% over the strongest CoT-RFT baseline while invoking a verifier on only 12.13% of samples and preserving the general multimodal capabilities of the underlying MLLM. The open-source repository is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[RiO-DETRv2: Geometry-Aware Uncertainty Modeling Meets Real-Time Oriented Object Detection Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29159)**  `arXiv:2610.29159`  
  _Zhangchi Hu, Zilong He, Yifan Zhao, Xiangyu Yu, Jie Chen, Peixi Wu, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  RiO-DETR is the first real-time end-to-end transformer for oriented object detection, yet further localization gains require a more expressive box representation. Although distributional regression has succeeded in horizontal DETRs, extending it to oriented detection introduces geometry-misaligned boundary modeling and topology-inconsistent orientation refinement. We present RiO-DETRv2, a geometry-aware uncertainty modeling framework for real-time oriented object detection. To resolve the geometry mismatch, Object-Aligned Boundary Distribution Refinement represents box boundaries as probability distributions in an object-aligned coordinate system and progressively refines them across decoder layers. For better orientation topology consistency, we introduce Circular-to-Local Angular Residual Refinement that transitions from global periodic search to local fine-grained estimation. Unified OBB Quality-Aligned Learning further aligns classification, boundary and angular refinement with complete-OBB localization quality. Together, these designs improve localization without compromising efficiency, achieving a better balance between speed and accuracy. Specifically, RiO-DETRv2-l/x achieves 53.7/54.1 mAP50-95 on DOTA, outperforming RiO-DETR-l/x by 1.2/1.5 points with nearly unchanged computational cost. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SICAF: Time-Varying Focus Bottleneck for Self-Supervised Event-Based Optical Flow with Spiking Neural Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29158)**  `arXiv:2610.29158`  
  _Shuangming Yang, Qing He, Shangqi Guo, Badong Chen_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Event-based optical flow estimation achieves high temporal resolution and dynamic range by exploiting asynchronous events, while self-supervised learning provides an effective solution to the absence of ground-truth labels. Spiking neural networks (SNNs), as brain-inspired models, process information through asynchronous spikes, naturally aligning with optical flow events. However, the temporal irregularity across event streams hinders the compact extraction of spatiotemporal features, while the absence of explicit labels leads to spurious correlations, thereby increasing redundancy within latent representations. In this paper, we propose a novel 'Compactly Compress, Time-varying Focus' training strategy. We present SICAF (Spikes Information Compression And Focus) framework that formulates the learning process as a constrained optimization with temporal modulation. It encourages self-supervised SNNs to extract compact latent representations while preventing over-compression that would discard temporal saliency. To better enable feature selectivity across time steps, we design an estimator with learnable temporal attention, achieving context awareness with cross-time dependencies. Experimental results on several datasets demonstrate that SICAF achieves state-of-the-art performance in self-supervised SNNs, with a 8.14% improvement in AEE on the MVSEC dataset and a 13.49% increase in robustness under white-box adversarial attacks.
  </details>

- **[From Augmentation to Synthetic Evidence: Evaluating Generated Chest Radiographs at Dataset Intake](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29152)**  `arXiv:2610.29152`  
  _Shaikh Akib Shahriyar, Zinedine Sehili, Adam Arthur, Christopher Schwartz_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  A generated chest radiograph can be useful as declared augmentation, but the same image becomes unsupported evidence when submitted as the record of a real examination. We study this problem by separating three questions: whether an image passes an automated quality check, whether an origin classifier flags it as generated, and whether a trusted record supports the claimed acquisition. In our experiments, Qwen selects up to 20 images from pools of 1,000 existing Cheff-identified or SynthCheX candidates. A receiver evaluates quality with MedGemma and predicted origin with a classifier on frozen DINOv3 features. Quality-based selection raises quality passes from 12 to 19 of 20 for Cheff-identified images and from 8 to 17 for SynthCheX. We find no evidence that selection improves passage through both checks: no selected Cheff-identified image passes both, while uniform, quality, and quality-constrained surrogate selection produce 2, 1, and 2 SynthCheX passes. On unprocessed test images, the origin classifier flags 4,677 of 5,000 SynthCheX images, but it also misclassify 110 of 2,297 NIH (real) images and 568 of 957 genuine images from another collection as fake. Our results further show that JPEG compression reduces SynthCheX-versus-NIH AUROC from 0.987 to 0.841. Even without processing, 152 SynthCheX images pass both pixel checks. These checks can filter submissions, but they cannot verify an acquisition claim. We strongly advocate that source and intended use must be recorded and checked separately for admission of chest radiographs in medical datasets.
  </details>

- **[DGCM-SSFA: Dual-Granularity Cross-Modulation and Sparse Strip Frequency Attention for Efficient 3T-to-7T MRI Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29141)**  `arXiv:2610.29141`  
  _Zhichun Fan, Zhiqiang Chen, Lanling Liu, Lingjun Liu, Zhonghua Xie_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Synthesizing high-fidelity 7T MRI from routinely acquired 3T scans offers a practical pathway to enhanced neuroimaging without the prohibitive cost of ultra-high-field scanners. While existing deep learning approaches have achieved promising results, state-of-the-art methods typically rely on either large-scale architectures exceeding 45 million parameters or computationally intensive ensemble strategies, limiting their practicality in resource-constrained clinical environments. In this work, we propose a compact multi-domain attention framework, termed DGCM-SSFA, that achieves competitive synthesis quality with only 11M parameters. The proposed architecture integrates two complementary modules into a U-Net Transformer backbone. First, a Dual-Granularity Cross-Modulated Feature Modulator (DGCM) is embedded at multiple hierarchical levels to enhance local edge and texture details through Sobel-guided spatial attention and depthwise frequency-aware channel attention with cross-branch gating. Second, a Sparse Strip Frequency Attention (SSFA) module at the bottleneck captures global spatial context via strip-pooled attention with near-linear complexity, while simultaneously performing FFT-based spectral modulation in the frequency domain. These two modules operate synergistically with the backbone's native channel attention, forming a triple-granularity feature refinement pipeline. Extensive experiments on the UNC and BNU 3T-to-7T MRI datasets across T1w and T2w modalities demonstrate that DGCM-SSFA achieves strong structural fidelity, attaining an SSIM of 0.8431 on BNU T2w---approaching the best benchmark value of 0.8624---and 0.8100 on BNU T1w, while using substantially fewer parameters than Transformer-based alternatives such as ResViT (123.4M) and FS-RWKV (45.4M).
  </details>

- **[WaFIR: Wavelet-Fourier Mixture-of-Experts with Subband-Adaptive Routing for Blind All-in-One Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29108)**  `arXiv:2610.29108`  
  _Trong Nhan Nguyen, Vu Hoang Minh Doan, Thanh Dat Le, Truong Tien Vo, Thi Thu Ha Vu, Tan Hung Vo, et al._
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Blind all-in-one image restoration must adapt a shared model to heterogeneous and sometimes mixed degradations without a task label at test time. We introduce WaFIR, a decoder module that decomposes features into Haar subbands, summarizes each subband with a compact Fourier descriptor, and routes it to prompt-conditioned spectral modulation experts. Unlike image-level frequency conditioning, WaFIR makes routing decisions at the subband level, enabling frequency-specific processing while retaining the task context learned by the backbone. Under a unified training and evaluation protocol for denoising, deraining, and dehazing, WaFIR improves average PSNR over the three tasks by 0.48 dB over the PromptIR baseline and by 0.38 dB over the recent Up-Restorer. Matched-capacity and routing controls show that the gain is not explained by additional parameters alone. On unseen noise intensities, descriptor-based routing is more robust than a discrete label-conditioned alternative and remains on par under compound degradations, while routing analyses show stable subband-dependent expert use across degradation types. These results support subband frequency structure as an interpretable complement to prompt-based conditioning for blind image restoration. Code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[PhyMo: Learning Physical Dynamics with Accurate and Continuous Motion from Multi-View Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29107)**  `arXiv:2610.29107`  
  _Shangjia Liu, Jinxi Li, Siyuan Zhou, Bo Yang, Bing Wang_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recovering dynamic 3D scene geometry, appearance, and the physical motion states that govern scene evolution from multi-view videos is important yet challenging. A key difficulty is that appearance can be deceptive. Under rendering supervision alone, a model may fit the observed images by changing color or opacity to compensate for inaccurate deformation, rather than recovering the true evolving geometry and motion. This issue is especially severe in scenes with fast or locally complex motion, where inaccurate trajectories can still produce plausible images over the observed frames, but lead to unreliable reconstruction and discontinuous future prediction. In this paper, we propose PhyMo, a framework for learning evolving dynamics from multi-view videos. We introduce a Motion-Aware Trajectory Representation that drives the model to recover accurate motion from deformation, rather than relying on appearance shortcuts to explain observed motion. On top of this, we propose a Continuous Motion Evolution module with bidirectional kinematic continuity constraint to promote temporally consistent evolution of displacement, velocity, and acceleration. Together, these designs yield geometrically faithful interpolation and continuous motion extrapolation. Experiments on four dynamic scene benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art performance.
  </details>

- **[DriftNeRF: Cross-Ray Density Drifting for Sparse-View Neural Radiance Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29106)**  `arXiv:2610.29106`  
  _Juntang Wang, Yihan Wang, Sheng'en Li, Hao Wu, Dongmian Zou, Shixin Xu_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Neural Radiance Fields (NeRF) synthesize photorealistic novel views from dense image collections, but degrade sharply when few views are available. We attribute a specific, measurable defect to the objective itself: the rendering loss is a sum over independent rays and contains no term comparing densities that different rays assign to the same region of space, so nearby 3D points can settle on contradictory densities while every training pixel is reproduced correctly. We make this quantity explicit by measuring cross-ray density disagreement directly during training, and show that it stays high in vanilla NeRF, falls only incidentally under frequency-based regularization, and moves together with held-out quality in the sparse regime. We then close the gap with DriftNeRF, a drop-in regularizer that instantiates the drifting-field template on density samples: a masked exponential kernel aggregates densities from nearby points on other rays into a stop-gradient consensus target, and the resulting drifting field penalizes unsupported disagreement while leaving agreed-upon boundaries intact. The regularizer reads only sampled positions, ray identifiers, and predicted densities, so it requires no pretrained model, external geometry, or additional images, and transfers across field parameterizations. On NeRF-Synthetic it reduces residual disagreement by an order of magnitude, and across NeRF-Synthetic, DTU, and LLFF it improves 3-view PSNR over the strongest MLP-NeRF baseline by 0.42-0.65 dB and remains competitive with sparse-view Gaussian pipelines under a matched protocol without their depth, flow, or covisibility preprocessing.
  </details>

- **[Diffusion Posterior Sampling with Uncertain Forward Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29102)**  `arXiv:2610.29102`  
  _Wentao Shangguan, Vivek K Goyal_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion posterior samplers reconstruct signals by combining a pretrained diffusion prior with a measurement likelihood specified by a forward model and a noise model. Calibration and reference data can characterize uncertainty in the forward model's parameters, such as blur kernels or detector responses. Fixing these parameters at estimated values leaves their uncertainty out of the likelihood, and parameter errors are thus absorbed into the reconstruction. We introduce an uncertainty-aware likelihood (UAL), a Gaussian approximation to the marginal likelihood obtained by integrating over the uncertain parameters. For each candidate signal, UAL propagates parameter uncertainty into a structured measurement covariance, reducing the quadratic penalty along residual directions that parameter variation can explain. Existing samplers can use UAL in their data updates while retaining their pretrained diffusion priors and sampling schedules. Under a fixed Gaussian nuisance model, we bound likelihood error from linearization and average divergence between the exact marginal and UAL posteriors. We compare samplers with and without UAL across six inverse problems spanning Gaussian and motion deblurring, CT, MRI, ultrasound, and fluorescence microscopy, and find improvements in reconstruction and ensemble prediction. UAL reduces geometric mean MSE by 9% to 20% across samplers in the main Gaussian-deblurring setting and by 51% to 92% in motion deblurring when non-blind samplers use the population-mean kernel. In motion deblurring, DAPS and PnP-DM with UAL also give 6.2 to 6.4 dB higher ensemble-mean PSNR than published BlindDPS and Fast Diffusion EM. On the ablation set, UAL outperforms trace-matched isotropic inflation for PnP-DM in both deblurring tasks; in motion deblurring, it also exceeds the best-tested fixed scalar data weighting by 3.3 dB for PnP-DM and 4.2 dB for DAPS in average PSNR.
  </details>

- **[UNIVERS: Learning UNIversal Voxel-wise Embedding via Randomized Synthesis for generalizable one-shot landmark localization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29098)**  `arXiv:2610.29098`  
  _Derong Yu, Qingsong Zhao, Yanbing Yue, Yikai Li, Zhi Zhang, Guoyan Zheng_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  One-shot landmark localization in volume scans with varying fields of view (FOVs) is challenging. While existing self-supervised learning methods have shown promising performance, they rely on contrastive learning built upon augmented views of the same image, which inherently limits their generalization in cross-modality scenarios. This motivates research on cross-modality embedding learning using paired multi-modality images. However, existing methods suffer from not only limited training data, but also the requirement of multi-stage iterative training. To address these limitations, we propose UNIVERS, an end-to-end trainable framework that learns UNIversal Voxel-wise Embedding via Randomized Synthesis. Our framework leverages diverse pseudo-data synthesized from organ shape templates, with randomization applied to semantic layout, visual appearance, FOV, and spatial pose. Coupled with cross-domain discriminative voxel-wise contrastive learning, this domain randomization strategy forces the network to disregard domain-specific biases and learn features that are robust against variations in imaging contrast, resolution, and spatial context. Consequently, the trained model enables one-shot landmark localization in multi-modality volume scans with arbitrary FOVs. In this study, the efficacy of the proposed method is demonstrated by applying it to the task of localizing the adrenal glands, which are paired, small, and irregular endocrine organs. In particular, comprehensive experiments are conducted on three challenging adrenal gland datasets. The experimental results demonstrate that UNIVERS consistently outperforms the state-of-the-art (SOTA) one-shot landmark localization approaches, with particularly prominent advantages in cross-modality scenarios. The source code is publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[ReSeg: Retraining-free segmentation of abdominal organs via generalizable semantic representation learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29090)**  `arXiv:2610.29090`  
  _Derong Yu, Guoyan Zheng_
  <details><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Abdominal organ segmentation is essential in modern medical applications. While existing deep learning approaches demonstrate promising performance, their heavy reliance on large-scale image data raises concerns over generalization capability and privacy risks. This motivates the development of generalizable segmentation training paradigms based on domain-randomized image synthesis, facilitating generalization across unseen real image domains. However, existing paradigms either suffer from limited segmentation performance when applied to abdominal organs, or require dataset-specific finetuning. To address these limitations, we propose ReSeg, a novel two-stage retraining-free framework for domain-agnostic zero-shot abdominal organ segmentation. At the first stage, our organ-agnostic synthetic data-driven pretraining approach exploits semantically and visually diverse synthetic images generated by a dedicated data engine to optimize a Generalizable Semantic Representation Network (GSRNet).
